Reproduced end-to-end: a real continue delivered onto B-Stack Max, the CLI answers with another `rate_limit_event status:"rejected"`, the breaker records `fails:[{key:SPARE}]` and spends its exhaustion notice — then ONE `status:"allowed"` reading through `recordRateLimitEvent` and the record is gone from memory *and* from `data/auto-resume.json` (`fires: {}`), `canFire(SPARE,'now')` answers `ok:true`, and the identity that had just rejected our continue is immediately fireable again. So neither budget the round-1 spec requires was ever enforced in production — the loop the breaker exists to break, one reading later. Fix: `noteRecovered(id, why, { worked = true } = {})`, and `noteFireOutcome(id, true, why)` runs **only when `worked`**. The DISARM stays unconditional for every caller (a fire onto a session that is no longer waiting is the wasted billed turn this call has always existed to prevent) — only the breaker reads the classification.

**Who may clear the loop breaker — every `noteRecovered` caller, classified.** The table is executable: test-auto-resume-loop §5 DERIVES the call sites (walk `src/`, keep whatever mentions `noteRecovered`, paren-match each call, read its `why` string and whether it passes `worked:false`) and fails on any site missing from the table *and* on any dead row — a hand-written inventory is the tool this codebase has watched fail four times (B-3185 r7).

| call site | `why` | worked | because |
|---|---|---|---|
| usage-pool-engine `noteTurnEnd` | `turn completed normally` | **yes** | the turn ended with real output. This is also how a *successful continue* clears the breaker: its own turn completes and reaches this line |
| ws-handler chat-input | `user sent a prompt` | **yes** | a human took the conversation over — exactly who the budget was protecting |
| usage-pool-engine `recordRateLimitEvent` | `fresh non-rejected reading` | no | a bucket has room; nothing about this conversation. The ~20×-per-rejection event that caused this defect |
| usage-pool-engine `recordCodexQuotaSignal` | `fresh non-limited codex reading` | no | the codex twin of the same reading |
| usage-pool-engine `reset_credit_result` | `codex reset credit consumed` | no | the LIMIT moved on that identity; the conversation still produced nothing. Nothing on this path fires (the disarm above already removed the wait), so the classification only decides whether a live quarantine survives — and it self-expires in 10min, the same floor `tryResetCredit` itself paces on |
| auto-resume `tick` | `disabled` | no | the feature was switched off under a live arm; a toggle off/on must not hand the loop a fresh budget any more than a deploy may |

**Round-4 invariants.**
- **A "the session is fine now" signal is not one signal.** Before letting a call clear state that BOUNDS SPENDING, ask what its cheapest caller actually knows — the passive readings outnumber the rejections ~20:1 here, so the rare caller's semantics were being written by the common one.
- **Disarm and de-quarantine are different decisions with different evidence bars.** Dropping a wait is cheap and self-correcting; forgetting that an identity rejected our last continue re-opens a billed loop. One function may serve both, but only if each half reads its own evidence.
- **The classification must be enforced where it is DECLARED, not trusted.** `worked` defaults to true (so the two proof-of-work callers stay unchanged), which means a new caller that says nothing gets the dangerous answer — acceptable only because the suite derives every call site and fails on an unclassified one before it can reach production.
- Negative control = the pre-fix CALL, replayed through a public seam: the engine reaches auto-resume through `getAutoResume()`, so handing it a wrapper that drops the third argument re-creates round 3 exactly, with both real producers untouched. With one reading per cycle it re-fires **25/25** cycles (≥10 = unbounded); with the classification honoured the same world stops at ≤3 and the quarantine holds. Mutation-tested three ways: the `if (worked)` guard removed ⇒ 13 asserts red (incl. `fires=25`); the claude call site's `{worked:false}` removed ⇒ 12 red (the codex legs stay green, so each call site is separately load-bearing, and the audit pin names the unlisted site); and the guard-removal is also run from a MUTATED COPY of the product source inside the suite so the guard itself has a leg that dies with it.

**Gates.** scripts/test-auto-resume-loop.mjs (186; each round-2/round-3/round-4 fix mutation-tested — re-applying the earlier behaviour turns 1–19 asserts red, including the exact round-1 card text) + scripts/test-auto-resume.mjs (128; §11 was rewritten to RECORD the refuted plan-B rule rather than delete it) + test-pool-auto (67) + test-account-pool (33) + test-account-verdicts (45) + test-otel-truth (43) + test-rate-limit-capture (42) + test-codex-pool (61).

## THE INSTANCE DEFAULT ATE THE CONVERSATION'S OWN VALUE ON EVERY RESUME (B-6b6d, owner ruling 2026-09-07 — option ①)

**What it was.** 2.369.62 fixed the record that STATED a turn's effort but disclosed the reason the wrong value was there in the first place: `src/lib/session-lifecycle.js` resolved `sessionEffort = effort !== undefined ? effort : defaults.effort` (and the identical `sessionModel` line) on EVERY create — including the ones that carry a `resumeId`. So a resume that supplied nothing (the chat resume bar, resume-all, a card with no ⚙ override, `restartConversationInPlace`, a fork) shipped `codex.defaultEffort` on the wire, ws-create's B-21e4 continuity fallback was gated on `if (!sessionSpec.env.CODEX_WEBUI_EFFORT)` — i.e. on the client having sent nothing — and therefore could not fire exactly when it was needed. A conversation the owner had set to `ultra` resumed at `xhigh`, and the wrapper then synthesized a `turn_context` saying so.

**The ruling.** The conversation's OWN value wins on resume/fork/restart. The instance default (`<prefix>.defaultModel` / `.defaultEffort`) is a NEW-session default. An explicit per-session pick (card ⚙, Session Properties, the New Session dialog's pickers) still beats both.

**The shape.** One PURE module, `src/resume-continuity.js` — `resumeSpawnPick({explicit, conversation, instanceDefault, resume, hasSource})` → `{value, origin}` with `origin ∈ chosen | conversation | instance | harness` — and TWO callers:
- **the client** (session-lifecycle's `createSession`) calls it with `hasSource:false`, which is the honest client-side fact: only the server can read a conversation's own records. On a continuation it resolves to `''` and the client sends NOTHING. `permission`/`extraArgs` deliberately keep master's behaviour (the ruling covers model+effort; a permission mode IS recoverable from claude's init record, so changing it is its own product decision ⇒ owner gate).
- **the server** (ws-create, immediately BEFORE `buildSessionArgs`) calls it with the conversation's value read through the harness descriptor's `store.lastTurn<Knob>` hook, and writes the resolved values back onto `data.model`/`data.effort` so every downstream reader (pool chooser's declared model, `_spawnModel`, the model lock's implicit target, `session._effort`) sees the value the session really starts with.

**`hasSource` is not a second declaration.** It is derived from the descriptor hook's PRESENCE, so a harness either ships the reader or it does not — there is no `resumeContinuity` caps boolean to drift out of sync with it. codex ships both (`lastTurnModel`/`lastTurnEffort` → the rollout's last `turn_context`, the B-21e4 readers moved onto the descriptor); **claude declares NEITHER** (round 2 — round 1 shipped a model reader and it is removed, see below); **opencode declares the model** (`serve.facts().sessionModel` — OpenCode's own session record names it, v1 list only so a resume adds no route the sidebar poll does not already use; `''` when the opt-in background service is off, which falls to the instance default and is logged). A generic ACP harness declares neither, and `src/harnesses/acp.js` says why in place rather than leaving it to be re-derived.

**THE ASYMMETRY, stated because it looks like an omission.** On a knob with NO conversation source, a resume sends **nothing** rather than the instance default — nothing claude writes records the effort a turn ran at, so an unconditional fallback there would mean "the instance default applies to every claude resume", which is the defect itself wearing a fallback's clothes. With a source that came up EMPTY (a thread resumed before its first turn) the instance default IS honest, and the ladder's caller logs the decision (`continuityLogLine`) so a resume that quietly took the default is readable afterwards.

**The origin is STATED, never derived.** A conversation's own value and the instance default are frequently the same string, and only the server ever read the conversation's records — so the panel cannot work out where a value came from. On a NEW session the client resolves the instance default itself, and the wire cannot tell that apart from a pick, so it also sends `spawnOriginHint` and the server reconciles through `applyOriginHint` (round 3 ②). The server records `_modelOrigin`/`_effortOrigin` on the session, persists them in session-meta (all three boot-restore paths — and every writer that RE-AUTHORS one persists it too, round 3 ①), and ships them on the `created` reply (the creator never gets an `attached` — 2.368.4), the attach payload, and the active-sessions payload. `spawnValueOrigin(stated, live, picked)` in agent-meta only decides how to SHOW it: a pick saved AFTER the spawn still flips the row to `spawn`, so the origin can never contradict the "(saved: X — applies on the next resume)" note beside it, and a session that predates the field falls back to `responseStyleOrigin`'s comparison rather than claiming a fact we do not have.

**Two twins fixed on the way past.** (a) `outputStyle` was added to the active-sessions payload in 2.369.58 but never to the sidebar MERGE, so Session Properties' "live" response-style value was silently always empty and every session read as `saved`; the B-6b6d rows land in both merge branches WITH it. (b) On the `created` reply the effort now rides along with the origin, because on a resume the client deliberately sends none — without it the chip on a resumed window would stay empty.

**Boundaries, stated rather than discovered later.** ① A REMOTE resume reads the conversation's records out of the local transcript CACHE (both locators already scan `data/remote-jsonl`), so its answer is as fresh as the last fetch — still the conversation's own value, just possibly an older one; the alternative (an unrelated instance default) is strictly worse, and this is the behaviour the codex twin has had since 2.369.32. ② The read costs one directory scan + one bounded tail read per resume, and only when the client sent NO pick — an explicit pick short-circuits it, because the ladder would discard the answer anyway. ③ `permission` and `extraArgs` still take the instance default on a resume: the ruling covers model+effort, and a permission mode IS recoverable (claude's init record), so moving it is its own product decision.

**Invariants.** ① An instance default is a NEW-object default; on a continuation, the object's own recorded value wins, and an explicit per-object choice wins over both. ② A fallback gated on "the caller sent nothing" is dead code for as long as some other layer always sends something — when you add such a gate, name the layer that must stay silent and pin it. ③ Do not declare the same capability twice: derive "can this harness answer?" from the presence of the reader, never from a parallel boolean. ④ Where a value came from is a FACT the resolver holds and no one downstream can reconstruct — state it on the record instead of guessing from the value. ⑤ A knob with no recoverable history must send nothing rather than a default, or the default becomes the answer for every continuation. ⑥ A row that states two facts must not let the ellipsis eat the second one (375×667: the origin is the last thing on the line — measured, then wrapped).

**ROUND 2 (adversarial verifier: 1 major + 4 medium/low, every one reproduced on real data first).**

**① THE CLAUDE MODEL SOURCE IS GONE, NOT REPAIRED (major + medium, one root cause).** Round 1's reader answered a different question from the one the ladder asks. The ladder asks *what should this resume COMMAND*; `message.model` says *which model SERVED that turn*, and it says it in a form that cannot express the conversation's context-window variant. Measured over the whole local corpus — 2650 transcripts, 3.5 GB under `~/.claude/projects` — **zero** `"model":"…"` values carry a `[…]` suffix, while the CLI's own `system/model_refusal_fallback` records prove conversations running `claude-fable-5[1m]` (5 sightings). So `--model claude-fable-5` on the resume of a `claude-fable-5[1m]` conversation silently turns 1M of context into 200k — on a path where master commanded nothing at all, since the client had just stopped sending `claude.defaultModel` there. It was also wrong about the model itself: a classifier reroute is recorded as an ASSISTANT record whose `message.model` is ALREADY the fallback target and which carries a `{type:'fallback',from,to}` content block, and the `system/model_refusal_fallback` record round 1's guard looked for is written **2–10 lines AFTER it** — so the ≤20-line BACKWARD look-back matched **1 of 139** reroutes in the owner's transcript, while **13,694 of 81,707** main-thread assistant records (16.8%, median run 87, max 1647) sit inside such a reroute run, i.e. ~1 in 6 interruption points would have resumed pinned to the fallback (and `_spawnModel`/`familyOfModel` would then have sent the plan-C pool chooser after an Opus-quota account for a Fable session). Round 1's own suite stayed green because its fixture wrote the system record BEFORE the assistant record it describes. **Fixing the guard would have left the variant loss**, and a rung that can never produce a commandable answer must not ship as the capability (the 2.369.66 rule), so the SOURCE goes: `lastClaudeTurnModel` and the claude descriptor's `store.lastTurnModel` are deleted, a claude resume commands NEITHER knob, and the CLI's own session record — which is variant-exact — decides. The asymmetry stops being an odd exception and becomes one sentence: **claude records neither knob in a form a spawn can command.** The reason lives in `src/session-store.js` where the reader used to be, so the next person to want one reads it first.

**② AN EXPLICIT "Auto (model default)" MUST SURVIVE (medium).** The New Session dialog always sends a defined string and its FIRST option is `''` (“Auto (model default)” / “Default”). Round 1 collapsed `''` into “no pick” in both tiers, so choosing Auto resolved to the instance default — measured: `{model:'', effort:''}` with `codex.defaultEffort=xhigh` reached `buildSessionArgs` as `effort:'xhigh'`, where master spawned with no flag at all. `''` is now a STATED choice on a NEW session (`{value:'', origin:'chosen'}`) and the client sends the resolved value VERBATIM there so the empty string survives the wire; ws-create forwards `explicit` untouched instead of normalising it. On a CONTINUATION the distinction genuinely is not available (the create message carries `model: sessionModel || undefined` on that path) and `''` still means the same as silence — documented in the module rather than left to be rediscovered.

**③ OPENCODE CAN ANSWER, SO IT DOES (medium).** With no `lastTurnModel`, an OpenCode resume stopped applying `opencode.defaultModel` and — unlike the claude-effort asymmetry, which is stated in three places — nothing took its place and nothing said so. OpenCode's own session record names the model, so the store now ships the reader (`sessionModel`, built on the same cached v1 `listNow` the sidebar `discover` uses: no new route, no per-directory instance — the 2.369.50 rule). The background service is opt-in and default OFF, so `''` is the common answer, which takes the instance default and logs which rung it used. The setting's own description says all of this.

**④ AN ORIGIN WE DO NOT HAVE IS NOT SHOWN (low).** `spawnValueOrigin(null, live, undefined)` fell through to `responseStyleOrigin`, which returns `'instance'` for any live value with no saved pick — so every session restored from an older session-meta asserted “(instance default)” as fact, including ones whose model came from the New Session dialog, and the helper's own comment said it declined to claim one. With no stated origin the helper may now only claim what the PICK proves (`spawn` / `saved` / `chosen`); otherwise it answers `'unknown'` and Session Properties renders the value with **no parenthetical**.

**Round-2 invariants.** ⑧ A reader that cannot express the thing it is read back INTO is not a source — measure the field's value space before wiring it to a spawn, and delete the source rather than stacking a second guard on it. ⑨ A guard whose window is derived from an assumed record ORDER must be built from records in the order the producer really writes them; a fixture that encodes the assumption will keep it green forever. ⑩ `''` and absent are the same thing only where the transport cannot tell them apart — say where that is, and do not collapse them anywhere else. ⑪ “No source ⇒ send nothing” is right, but it silently changes behaviour for every harness that acquires it, so each one either gets a real reader or gets the sentence written down where its own descriptor lives. ⑫ A helper that declines to claim a fact must not delegate to one that will.

**ROUND 3 (adversarial verifier: 2 medium + 1 low, each reproduced against the real module before it was touched).**

**① THE RE-AUTHORED ORIGIN WAS NEVER PERSISTED (medium).** `src/server/stdout/codex-events.js` re-authors `session._effortOrigin` when the wrapper reports an effort we did not command (`'conversation'` — the thread's own) or one that moved away from a value we did (`'chosen'` — a `/effort` typed inside the session), but its `writeSessionMeta` listed `effort` and not `effortOrigin`. Reproduced on the real consumer through `src/server/session-stdout.js` with the spawn state ws-create really persists for a codex resume whose rollout said `ultra` (`{effort:'ultra', effortOrigin:'conversation'}`, verified on disk): after `/effort high` memory says `high / chosen` and the DISK said `high / conversation`, and `boot-restore.js` reads `meta.effortOrigin` — so after a restart Session Properties rendered `high (this conversation's own value)` for a value the user had just changed by hand. That is verbatim the contradiction the origin exists to remove; the field's own schema row claimed `persisted:'meta'`, which was only half true. The write now lists `effortOrigin`. It deliberately does **not** list `modelOrigin`: nothing in this consumer authors that key, the object spread above already carries it through, and re-listing a key a writer does not own would stamp `null` over a real disk value for any session object that lacks the field — pinned as a negative control rather than assumed.

**② A NEW SESSION COULD ONLY EVER RECORD 'chosen' (medium).** For a NEW create the CLIENT resolves `<prefix>.default*` (it needs the value for its own chip, and it owns two facts the server does not: the legacy `session.defaultEffort` key, honoured only while `<prefix>.defaultEffort` is UNMODIFIED, and `settings.isModified` itself) and — since round 2 — sends it VERBATIM so a stated `''` survives. The server therefore saw a bare string it could only read as an explicit pick: `_modelOrigin`/`_effortOrigin` were **always** `'chosen'` on a new session, `'instance'` and `'harness'` were unreachable there, and Session Properties labelled `<prefix>.defaultModel` “your choice for this session” on every create path that shows no picker at all — `openShellTerminal`, the toolbar, `setup-flows`, `manage-agents` (the branch's own browser leg printed the evidence: `wire-none {model:'gpt-5.6-sol', effort:'xhigh'}` for a create that supplied nothing). The fix is not a guess: the client already computed the origin and threw it away, so it now rides along as `spawnOriginHint` and `applyOriginHint(origin, hint)` (PURE, in the same module) reconciles. It may only **downgrade** our own `'chosen'` to `'instance'`/`'harness'`; every other rung was decided by the server from facts the client never had, the value is untouched, a hint of `'conversation'`/`'chosen'`/garbage is ignored, and a client that sends none keeps master's answer.

**③ A CLAUDE RESUME LOST ITS POOL PLACEMENT (low).** Round 2 deleted the claude model source, so `data.model` is undefined on a claude resume — and the pooled-spawn chooser (`poolChooserForModel`) short-circuits on `familyOfModel(null)` **before** `decidePoolSwitch`, placing the session on the pool's current target with no evaluation at all. Reproduced on the REAL engine with a REAL `AccountManager` pool and real caches in the 2.305.0 shape (5h/7d fine, the current target's Opus weekly cap spent): master's input placed the session on the healthy member, the branch stranded it on the spent one — and a claude pool switch does not reach a running CLI for ~25 min (2.361.0), so the spawn-time placement is the one that counts. **Placement is not commanding**: the chooser only wants a model FAMILY (`'fable'`/`'opus'`/`'sonnet'` — coarse by construction, no point release and no `[1m]` variant, which is exactly why the variant argument that killed the reader does not apply here). `data._placementModelHint = data.model || <prefix>.defaultModel || null` restores the input master had and reaches the chooser call site and nothing else — never `data.model`, never the spawn spec, never the argv (pinned). With the setting unset the hint is `null` and behaviour is master's. **`session._spawnModel` is deliberately left null on that path** and must stay so: it records what the spawn COMMANDED, and the spawn commanded nothing — writing the hint there would put a value we never sent into the attach payload, session-meta, the restart chip and `sessionModelFor`'s floor. The running session gets its real answer from `_servedModel` at the first assistant record; the gap the hint closes is the one moment before that, which is exactly the placement decision.

**Round-3 invariants.** ⑬ When one tier resolves a value the other tier must classify, the wire has to be able to carry *which fact it is* — a bare value is indistinguishable from a choice, and the reconciliation belongs in the same pure rule as the resolution (a hint may only weaken the receiver's own verdict, never re-point a fact only the receiver holds). ⑭ A writer persists the keys it AUTHORS and leaves the rest to the spread; listing a key you do not own is a silent `null` over somebody else's fact, and the difference is a negative control, not a comment. ⑮ Deleting a source removes it from every consumer, including the ones that only needed a WEAKER projection of it — before removing a reader, enumerate the readers of its output and ask what each of them actually needs (family vs exact id). ⑯ An ordering assert must first assert that both sites exist: `indexOf` answers `-1` for absent text and `-1` is less than everything, so the pin goes green exactly when the code it orders has been deleted (measured — this assert survived its own mutation run before it was fixed).

**Gates.** scripts/test-codex-effort-meta.mjs 245 (leg ⑪ = the PURE truth table incl. the stated-empty rule, the end-to-end rollout→descriptor→ladder→adapter-env→REAL wrapper run whose FIRST synthesized `turn_context` already says `ultra`, the pre-fix client replayed as the negative control, the entry-point audit, and the origin plumbing; leg ⑪e = round 2 — the claude reroute/variant DATA pins built from the real record order with round 1's fixture order as the negative control, the claude “neither knob” behaviour, the stated-empty create, the opencode reader against a fake serve, and the `'unknown'` origin row; round 3 = leg ⑦'s second bench (the real stdout consumer with the real spawn state on disk, the restored-vs-live claim compared through the real label rule, and `modelOrigin` surviving the write untouched), leg ⑫ (the `applyOriginHint` truth table + the client→JSON-wire→server composition, with the hint removed as the negative control that reproduces “everything is 'chosen'”), leg ⑫c (both halves pinned against source, incl. the placement hint never becoming a command) and leg ⑬ (the REAL pool engine: the fix places on the healthy member, the pre-fix chooser argument strands on the spent one, master's input agrees with the fix, and an unset setting is master's behaviour)) + test-auto-resume (129, the 2.368.4 `created`-payload law) + test-codex-0153 (93) + test-owner-batch-2369-32 (28) + test-architecture (82, the new PURE module) + test-pool-auto (67) / test-account-pool (33) / test-account-verdicts (45) / test-codex-pool (61) for round 3's placement leg + test-session-schema/test-ws-contract/test-harness-contract (132)/test-stdout-registry (58)/test-wrapper-files. Mobile: scripts/dbg-session-props-mobile.mjs (375×667, real server + headless chrome) — round 2 adds the pre-upgrade session (Model/Effort rows carry the value and ZERO dim spans, 18px vs the stated row's 32px, no overflow) and a WIRE leg that drives the real `createSession` in the page with `ws.send` swallowed (nothing reaches the server, no agent is spawned): a NEW create carries the stated empty verbatim, a create that supplied nothing carries the instance defaults, a continuation sends neither key. Round 3 adds the `spawnOriginHint` each of those four creates emits (`instance` / `chosen` / `harness` / `harness`) and the row the fix makes reachable at all — `'instance'` was unreachable for a new session, so “(instance default)” had never been rendered on a phone: both twins 18px, unclipped, `contentOverflowX`/`docOverflowX` = 0.

## THE MERGE ITSELF WAS THE DEFECT (integration r2, 2026-09-07: readings-by-slot + B-6b6d + login-expiry landing together)

Three findings from an adversarial review of the INTEGRATED tree. None of them exists on either chain alone — each is a property of the merge, and each was reproduced before it was fixed.

**① MAJOR — pre-filtering the evidence out of the decision degraded the notice (usage-pool-engine).** Chain A hardened `healthyPoolMembers` to drop any member `src/login-state.js` (the FILE reader: "can a request be made right now") calls unusable. Master 2.369.67 had, the same day, shipped the pool's login attribution — and it BUILDS that attribution from the members the decision was SHOWN: `decidePoolSwitch` pushes each login-blocked member into `loginBlocked` and only then may claim `all-logins-expired`. A member removed before the call can never be named. Reproduced on the doubly-expired shape (`accessToken` present, both `expiresAt` and `refreshTokenExpiresAt` in the past — the satisfiable gap `login-state.js`'s own header documents, and the one shape `parseAuth` still reports `loggedIn:true` for, so `poolMembers()` keeps it):

- master, unfiltered: `Pool "MyPool": no member can take it — Bravo (login expired), Charlie (login expired). Re-login those accounts in Manage Agents.`
- merged, pre-filtered: `Pool "MyPool": no member can serve it — spent: 5h 1% (still available: 7d 50%). Conversations on it will hit a limit until a window resets, you add a member, or you move them off the pool.`

i.e. the one wall a user clears in 30 seconds, reported as a spent quota bucket with the quota remedy — verbatim the defect 2.369.67 exists to fix. Both suites stayed green because **neither crosses the seam**: every `all-logins-expired` assert in test-login-expiry calls `decidePoolSwitch` DIRECTLY with a hand-built member list, and test-readings-attribution pinned only that `healthyPoolMembers` returns `[]` for an all-unusable pool, never what the notice then says.

**Fix — fold, do not pre-filter.** `poolReadLogin()` (the memoized reader every gate already consults) now asks BOTH modules and takes the worse answer: `accounts.loginStateOf` (src/login-expiry.js — the refresh-token DEADLINE) and `memberLoginState` (src/login-state.js — the FILE). It is only ever a DOWNGRADE, and it keeps the file reader's own numbers (`since` is the instant it stopped working, so `msLeft` stays a coherent negative number instead of an 'expired' state carrying "30 days left"). Decision sites take a new `switchCandidates(poolId)` — the 2.335.0 auth-fail escape, no login pre-filter. ACT sites (`notePoolAuthFailure`'s `alive`, `poolChooserForModel`) keep `healthyPoolMembers`' hard exclusion, because there the list is a set of re-point targets. **Exclusions are byte-identical; the attribution came back.** The fold is load-bearing, not cosmetic: the shape only the FILE reader can see (access token expired, refresh token GONE) reads 'unknown' = no claim to the deadline reader, so without it the naming would have cost the exclusion.

**② MEDIUM — a sub-line resurrection in the auto-loaded LAW index (CLAUDE.md).** The hand-merge resolved the Pool/billing routing cell by keeping "both sides verbatim", which took master's copy — still carrying base text chain A's round 3 had deliberately deleted. The cell then stated the refuted rule (`quota VALUES keep the observed-org routing`) twenty characters before its own refutation, while the shipped tree routes every value through `readingSlotFor` and leaves `setTruthLookup` unwired on purpose. The routing row is ONE 3.3 KB line, so the revert is SUB-LINE: a line-granularity lost/resurrected check reports zero. Fixed by deleting the superseded paragraph (the following one subsumes it and is strictly more correct); the historical kb essay keeps the rule in the PAST tense ("kept", not "keep") — that tense is the whole difference between a record and a rule.

**③ MEDIUM — a lying gate: the version-marker leg cleared a same-topic PREDECESSOR (test-codex-effort-meta ⑩).** The leg carried `MARK = '2.369.62'` as "this change's marker" and cleared any claimant matching `/effort|ultra|xhigh|turn_context/i`. Master had ALREADY RELEASED 2.369.62 — for this chain's own predecessor ("codex effort a TURN ran at … test-codex-effort-meta 108; B-6b6d filed for the resume-default half") — which matches that regex BY CONSTRUCTION, because the sites cite it precisely for being on that topic. So the assert went green while asserting something false. **A topic regex cannot separate a predecessor release from its own follow-up.**

The remedy was NOT a renumber: master's 2.369.62 is an ancestor of HEAD and every 2.369.62 in the tree is a correct BACK-REFERENCE to it, so rewriting them to an unreleased number would MANUFACTURE the dangling cross-reference the leg exists to prevent. The number was playing two roles the leg conflated, so it now asks two questions: ① the back-reference must RESOLVE (the release exists on the integration branch and is about the work the sites cite it for); ② this branch is unreleased and stamps no number of its own, so the leg names the first free number (the suite's `NEXT_FREE` constant — the only place it may be written, since a doc that hardcodes an unassigned number is the very cross-reference this leg forbids), PROVES it is unclaimed via git, and runs a census asserting every `2.369.x` named in the cited files is a release that really exists. `describesThisChange` was tightened to markers only this change carries (`resumeSpawnPick` / `resume-continuity` / `resume ladder` / `spawnOriginHint` / `readingSlotFor` / `readings-by-slot`) — deliberately NOT `B-6b6d`, which the predecessor's own subject already names.

**Invariants.**
- **Evidence must survive to the step that judges on it.** Removing a member before `decidePoolSwitch` removes what the refusal is able to SAY; fold a second verdict into the reader the gate already consults instead, and keep the hard exclusion only where the list is acted on.
- **Two suites that each pin one half of a seam are not a gate for the seam.** The crossing leg asserts the SERVER NOTICE STRING end to end (`healthyPoolMembers → decidePoolSwitch → poolBlockedNotice`), because that is the artifact the user reads.
- **The auto-loaded index is a source pin.** It gates the NEXT change, so a superseded paragraph there outranks any essay that contradicts it — and in a one-line table cell a resurrection is sub-line, invisible to line-granularity checks. Pin the row against the CODE that refutes it.
- **A version marker is a CROSS-REFERENCE: it either resolves on the integration branch or it is a defect.** One number cannot be both "a back-reference" and "this change's stamp" — one of the two halves will be false.
- **"Same topic" is not "same change."** A predecessor and its follow-up share every topic word; distinguish them with a marker only the follow-up carries.
- **An assertion that can pass vacuously is not an assertion.** Even "this number is free" has to be proven against git, or it degrades into a comment.

**Gates.** scripts/test-readings-attribution.mjs 194 (§5b = the crossing leg + three negative controls: an all-quota-dead pool must keep the quota sentence and never say re-login; the file-only-dead shape must be excluded AND named; the two lists must stay two different questions — plus a wiring pin, the 2.355.0 unstaged-wiring class. §9b = the LAW-index pin with the pre-fix row as its negative control, and an explicit allowance for the historical essay). scripts/test-codex-effort-meta.mjs 245 (⑩ rewritten: predecessor resolves / next number proven free / census over the cited files / three negative controls — the same-topic predecessor passing the old predicate and failing the new one is the permanent proof the blindness is gone).

## THE EFFORT A TURN RAN AT — OUR SYNTHESIZED TWIN OUTLIVED THE TRUTH (2.369.62, owner "我刚才把那个van的对话调成了 ultra, 但我看它回复怎么都 metadata 显示的是 xhigh?")

**Symptom.** A conversation the owner had set to `ultra` reported `xhigh` in the message-metadata popup on every single reply, for the whole session.

**What the evidence said.** codex's own rollout carries `turn_context.effort = ultra` on every turn since 2026-09-06T20:33 (21/21, plus its own `thread_settings_applied` records), the usage ledger says `ultra`, and `data/session-meta` said `ultra`. But the LIVE buffer of that session — 1137 records — contained exactly ONE `turn_context`: the wrapper's synthesized copy at `07:06:44.566Z`, effort `xhigh`. codex's own copy of the SAME turn says `ultra` at `07:06:46.484Z`.

**Root cause (three faults stacked).** ① The wrapper synthesizes the `turn_context` every reader takes a turn's effort from, at `turn/started` — and 0.153.4's `TurnStartedNotification` is `{threadId, turn:{id,status,…}}` with **no effort and no model**, so the value comes from our own bookkeeping. It printed the mutable `effort` var = the SPAWN ENV, which on this resume was the instance default `codex.defaultEffort = xhigh`. ② The turn was started by the APP-SERVER (an auto-continue on resume: `goal_cleared`, `turn/started`, then the `thread/resume` reply — all inside 07:06:44.565-.566Z), so we never sent `turn/start` and that value had never reached codex at all; the reply carrying `reasoningEffort:'ultra'` landed 1.9ms LATER and only assigned `meta.effort`, leaving the emitted record and the `effort` var alone (the adoption at updateMetaFromThread is gated on `!effort`). ③ That single record was the only `turn_context` in the buffer — the app-server steered and drained everything inside that one turn — so `CodexMessageManager._status.effort` stayed `xhigh` and every `token_count` baked `xhigh` onto every message. A fourth fault kept it from ever healing: `mergeCodexRecords` fingerprints a turn_context on its turn id and kept the FIRST copy, and ours is always the earlier of the twins — so codex's authoritative copy was suppressed on every rebuild, forever.

**Fix.** Three named facts replace one var in the wrapper (`effort` = commanded-next, `threadEffort` = the app-server's own word, `turnEffort` = what the ACTIVE turn runs at); a turn WE start uses what we put on `turn/start` (which also re-points the thread, per TurnStartParams' own doc, reverted on refusal), a turn the app-server starts uses the THREAD's; `noteTurnEffort()` RESTATES the same turn id when the resume reply lands late; `wrapper_meta` carries the `{effort, effortNext}` pair (mid-turn attach, no rollout re-read); `set-effort` now also sends `thread/settings/update {effort}` so a turn the app-server starts on its own runs at the value the UI is claiming; the merge folds a repeated turn_context into the first copy but takes the better copy's VALUES (codex's own always wins); and the normalizer splits `_status.effort` (this turn) from `_status.effortNext` (pending) with one writer that also pushes a live `meta/effort` op.

**Invariants.** ① A record that states what a turn IS RUNNING AT may only carry a value that reached the agent — the pending/commanded value belongs in its own field, and a pick made mid-turn never relabels messages the running turn already produced. ② When we synthesize a twin of a record the agent also writes, the agent's copy MUST win the fold — ours is structurally the earlier one, so first-wins silently makes our guess permanent. ③ A knob the UI presents as "applies from now on" must be told to the party that will act on it (`turn/start` alone is not enough when the app-server starts turns by itself). ④ A liveness/state check must read the process's own immediate statement, not a debounced sidecar — the 200ms `scheduleMeta` lag is enough to queue a message into the turn you just stopped (caught by this fix's own test bench). ⑤ 'ultra' is a MODE, not a level: state both facts from the catalog or state only the mode; never hardcode the level.

**Known residue — CLOSED by B-6b6d (owner ruling 2026-09-07, essay below). The paragraph is kept because it is the diagnosis the fix was built from.** `codex.defaultEffort` is documented as applying to "new **or resumed** Codex sessions", and the client sends it as `data.effort` at `src/lib/session-lifecycle.js` (`sessionEffort = effort !== undefined ? effort : defaults.effort`), which suppresses ws-create's B-21e4 EFFORT-CONTINUITY fallback (`lastCodexTurnEffort`) — whose whole purpose is that a resume "can never flip a conversation that was running at high/ultra". **Precise scope** (r2 review corrected the first wording, which read as if every resume were affected): the card's gear popover writes a per-session override and `resumeSession` forwards it, so the bite is limited to resume paths that supply nothing — the **chat resume bar**, **resume-all**, and any card with no saved override — where `savedCfg.effort` is `undefined` and the instance default takes its place. The **MODEL twin is the identical line** (`sessionModel` / `lastCodexTurnModel`, ws-create.js:394) and is unchanged for the same reason. Flipping the priority ("only apply the instance default when there is no `resumeId`") is a product-default decision ⇒ owner gate, and it must move effort and model together (STANDING SWEEP). What this release does instead is make it VISIBLE and stop it corrupting the record: the session's saved effort is now honest (the wrapper's own value reaches `session._effort` + session-meta, so a `/effort` typed into the chat no longer leaves a stale value behind for the next resume), and the status-bar tooltip says which value the turn in flight is really running at. Leg ⑪ pins both twins plus the mechanism itself (the adapter really does leave `CODEX_WEBUI_EFFORT` empty when nothing is supplied, and really does fill it when the default is), so a half-fix fails red.

**Round 2 (adversarial verifier; four defects, all reproduced before fixing).**
- **① The version marker named someone else's release (major).** The branch stamped `2.369.61` into 36 places across 17 files while master had ALREADY SHIPPED `2.369.61` (Alt+Enter = steer) — 16 seconds before this branch's own commit. Same class as the 2.369.58 r2 renumber off `2.369.54`. Renumbered to **2.369.62** in one pass. The belt that existed (test-harness-honesty's CHANGELOG rule) could not see it: a branch that is not rebased has no CHANGELOG entry for EITHER number, so the check passes vacuously. **The number is claimed on the INTEGRATION BRANCH, so the check has to ask git** — leg ⑩ scans `origin/master`'s release-commit subjects AND its CHANGELOG headings for the marker and fails unless the claimant IS this change, with the old number `2.369.61` as a standing negative control (it must still read as claimed-by-someone-else, or the check is blind). No ref ⇒ explicit SKIP, never a silent pass.
- **② `effortNext: null` is a statement, not a gap (minor).** The new session-meta writer read `payload.effortNext || payload.effort` — re-conflating the two facts the release exists to separate. Picking "Auto (model default)" makes the wrapper clear the thread's effort and publish `{effort:'<the last turn ran at>', effortNext:null}`; the fallback then wrote the last turn's LEVEL into `session._effort`, session-meta, the attach payload's `chatStatus.effortNext`, the chip after a restart and the next resume spawn — overwriting the `null` ws-handler had just written on the same click. Now `effortNext` is the only field read, and only when present (`undefined` = an old wrapper says nothing ⇒ master's behaviour, the 2.361.1/2.364.1 skew rule).
- **③ A record in codex's spelling that codex refused (minor).** `set-effort` emitted `thread_settings_applied` — codex's OWN rollout shape — unconditionally, including after `thread/settings/update` was rejected, and unmarked. Our payload carries 5 `thread_settings` keys where codex's real one carries 9 (`model_provider_id`, `approvals_reviewer`, `collaboration_mode`, `permission_profile`…), so the two can never share a fingerprint and a rebuilt history kept both forever. Now: recorded only when the RPC was ACCEPTED (a refusal leaves the per-turn `turn/start` fallback, and the pending pick still reaches every client through `wrapper_meta.effortNext` — a statement about US, which a refusal leaves true), and both wrapper-authored copies carry `wrapper: true` like the synthesized `turn_context`. `mergeCodexRecords` folds the twin the same way (codex's copy wins), keyed on the stated VALUES and scoped to ADJACENCY — high → ultra → high is a real sequence, and folding the third into the first would leave a rebuilt status reporting `ultra`.
- **④ The disclosed resume gap needed an owner, not a paragraph (minor).** Filed as B-6b6d with the MODEL twin; scope corrected above.

**Round-2 invariants.** ⑥ A version marker is a CROSS-REFERENCE: it must resolve on the branch it will be integrated into, and the only source that knows is git — a CHANGELOG check on an un-rebased branch is vacuous by construction. ⑦ `null` from a producer that always sends the field is a VALUE ("nothing pending"); only `undefined` is absence. Never `a || b` across two fields that mean different things — that is the exact conflation the incident is about, re-introduced one layer down. ⑧ Never write a record in another party's spelling for something that party did not do; if you must synthesize one, MARK it, and let the real author's copy win the fold.

Gate: scripts/test-codex-effort-meta.mjs (108) — the race reproduced against a stub app-server with the REAL wrapper, plus per-defect regression legs with negative controls (master's own record shapes; a REFUSING app-server; `effortNext:null` vs an old wrapper's absent field; adjacent-vs-sequential settings twins; the squatted version number).
## B-7638 — THE SEVEN REVIEW RESIDUALS (the 2026-09-06 review-fixes-r2 verify; every one reproduced before it was fixed)

Three fixes shipped in that batch each left something behind that the verify pass named and the repro confirmed. They are unrelated in code and identical in shape: **a rule was written for the state the incident happened to be in, and the state it drifts into afterwards was never asked about.**

**① The over-cap adoption trusted a 4 KB window (src/hosts.js).** The remote-transcript cache heals a pre-provenance slot by refetching it whole, with ONE exception: a remote already past `maxBytes` cannot be refetched at all, so a byte-verified cache adopts the provenance instead of failing "too large". "Byte-verified" read the last 4 KB — sound at the moment of a splice, because an append lands its foreign bytes at the TAIL. But the pre-fix delta path kept appending AFTER it spliced, so on any long-lived slot the marker is buried under later appends and a tail read calls the hybrid verified — forever, because a stopped thread never changes again. The whole-file scan (1 MiB chunks, 3-byte carry so a zstd magic split across a boundary is still seen) is the evidence such a slot owes. **Round 2 corrected WHICH slots owe it.** The first cut gated the deep scan on "the meta carries no `remotePath`" — but that is exactly the provenance the *shipped* tail-only adoption used to stamp: it adopted a buried hybrid and the delta path then wrote `remotePath` onto that very slot, so `sameRemote` is TRUE for the corruption the scan exists to find, and every slot that passed through the ⑥ window would have been served on a tail check forever. The gate is now a meta SCHEMA MARKER (`v: 2`) written by every meta writer in `_fetchRemoteByFind`: any meta from before this fix pays ONE whole-file scan the first time its bytes are trusted, and is re-stamped so the cost is paid once per slot, never per poll. A refused slot is left unstamped.

**① b The cap error's remediation could not work (src/hosts.js).** The same batch appended "delete it to re-sync" to the "too large" error when the cache did not verify. The suffix is reachable ONLY when the remote is past the cap — where a whole refetch is impossible by construction — so following the advice destroyed the last local copy and failed identically (verified: delete cache → same error; delete cache AND meta → same error). It now states the true situation: no prefix to grow from, the remote is past the *N* MB cap, and deleting the cache would not help. Advice in an error message is a claim about the system; it needs the same evidence as any other claim.

**② The ssh rung could not grow an over-cap slot (src/hosts.js).** Both early-return branches handed the verified cache back and left the meta unstamped, so the whole-file scan repeated on every poll; `stampVerified` (remotePath + compressed + `adopted` + the schema marker) on both rungs ends that — **and that is all the stamp does**. The round-2 verify caught the first cut claiming more: the *harm* here was that the legacy ssh rung hard-failed "remote transcript too large" on the very next byte of growth, and stamping provenance changes nothing on that path (the short-circuit needs `meta.size === size`, so any growth falls straight into the cap throw, with no fallback to the verified cache — a >64 MB transcript that opened yesterday was an error today). The real fault was a TWIN GAP: the slab rung has grown an over-cap slot by an append-only delta since 2.187.0 and the ssh rung only ever whole-`cat`ted, so the same conversation behaved differently depending on which transport happened to serve it. The ssh rung now carries the same delta (`tail -c +N`, 1-based on GNU and BSD), under the same legality test (same remote file, uncompressed on both sides, cached prefix no longer than the remote) and the same never-stamp-bytes-we-did-not-get rule — with one refinement the last rung needs: a read that OVERTAKES the stat (a live transcript grew mid-poll) keeps the extra bytes, because an append-only file's extra tail bytes are genuinely its next bytes, and stamps what the cache actually holds; only a SHORT read throws.

**② b ROUND 3 — the three residuals of the round-2 delta itself (src/hosts.js).** The verify that passed round 2 named them, and each was reproduced before it was fixed.
  **(i) The compression-legality clause had no negative control on either rung.** `canDelta`/`canDeltaSsh` both carry `!isZstPath(remotePath) && !cacheIsCompressed()`, and DELETING it left test-codex-zst ALL PASS — the suite only ever exercised the twin-SWITCH (plain ⇄ .zst), never a growth with BOTH sides compressed. That case is the innocent-looking one and the worst: a host re-compresses a finished rollout, so the remote keeps its path, the cache holds .zst bytes that verify (for a compressed slot the magic IS the evidence), and the cached archive is shorter than the remote — every other delta precondition holds. The "delta" would weld a foreign frame tail onto the old frames; `zstdDecompressFrames` then stops at the earlier frames and the slot serves the OLD transcript, with the growth silently gone. Both rungs now carry a both-compressed growth leg (⑥d) whose asserts go red when the clause is deleted from EITHER copy — the two legality tests are twins, and a guard only one copy carries is the drift this batch keeps paying for.
  **(ii) The delta REPLACED the last rung's whole `cat`.** Round 2 added `tail -c +N` to the ssh rung as an `if/else` against the whole-`cat` branch, so a `tail` that FAILS — the remote truncated/rotated between the stat and the read, or a host whose `tail` has no `-c +N` — now hard-fails a fetch the pre-r2 code completed, and this is the LAST rung: nothing under it to fall through to. A delta failure now falls back to the whole `cat` when `size <= maxBytes` (logging the failure VERBATIM, per the degrade-path rule), and the hard error is kept ONLY over the cap — where a whole re-pull is impossible by construction and the delta really was the only road, so the fallback can never become a cap bypass. The over-cap short-read hard failure from round 2 is unchanged.
  **(iii) The meta writers rebuilt provenance instead of carrying it.** Both rungs stamped a FRESH object at the end of a fetch, so the very next delta erased `adopted` (the marker meaning "these bytes were VERIFIED, never fetched") and the `slab` lane marker — the exact opposite of the durability ① claims, and it landed on the one slot class that can never re-earn it (an over-cap slot cannot be refetched to prove itself again). One shared `nextMeta(fields, {whole})` now spreads the prior meta on BOTH rungs; only a WHOLE refetch clears those two markers, because they describe bytes that are no longer in the slot.

**② c ROUND 4 — TWO POLLS, ONE SLOT, ONE OFFSET (src/hosts.js).** Both rungs are now read-then-append against a size measured BEFORE the remote read, and nothing serialized the SLOT. Two overlapping fetches of the same conversation — the 5s session poll and a user opening the window, two clients, goal-sync and an attach — each read `fs.statSync(cachePath).size`, each asked the host for `[localSize, size)`, and each appended it: the cache grew a DUPLICATED region. That much self-heals (a doubled file no longer matches its own meta ⇒ whole refetch), which is why three review passes walked past it; the permanent damage is one poll later. Once the remote grows past the doubled size the legality test holds again, the next tail is appended at the doubled offset, and the file's size now EQUALS the remote's — so the stamp says COMPLETE and the short-circuit serves it forever, with a region duplicated and the region behind it MISSING. Nothing catches it downstream either: duplicated JSON records carry neither a NUL byte nor a zstd magic, so the byte checks of ① are silent, and a stopped conversation never changes again. Two independent legs, because the failure has two independent sources. **(i) The slot is single-flighted**: `_fetchRemoteByFind` is now a thin wrapper over the core (`_fetchRemoteByFindOnce`) holding an in-flight promise per cache path — a second caller AWAITS the first's verdict instead of racing it (one remote read, one append; every caller resolves its cap from the same harness descriptor, so a coalesced caller cannot lose a cap it needed, and the next poll re-decides with its own). **(ii) The append refuses a stale offset**: `_appendDeltaAt(cachePath, offset, buf)` opens `r+`, re-reads the size THROUGH the fd it is about to write with, and writes POSITIONALLY at `offset` — at a matching offset the write is idempotent, at a mismatching one it does not happen at all. `false` means "the slot moved": under the cap that is a whole refetch on both rungs (the ssh rung reuses the round-3 fallback), over the cap — where a whole re-pull is impossible by construction — it is a loud failure that leaves the slot exactly as it was found. Pins: test-codex-zst ⑥f, with pre-fix repros for both legs (the doubling, then the poll that seals it and the short-circuit that then serves it forever) and mutation-tested one leg at a time: deleting the single-flight reddens the two coalescing asserts on both rungs, deleting the offset check reddens the four moved-slot asserts.

**② d ROUND 5 — THE REFUSAL THAT DEGRADED (src/hosts.js).** Round 4's moved-slot refusal was correct and unreachable on the rung that needed it most. The over-cap `throw` sits INSIDE the data-plane `try`, and that catch means "this TRANSPORT did not work, try the other one" — so on a dial host (or any host with the data plane on) with a reachable ssh fallback the refusal landed in `catch (e2) { /* legacy fallback below */ }`, silently, and the ssh rung then measured `fs.statSync(cachePath).size` on the MOVED file, computed a fresh tail offset from it and appended: the remote's bytes for the moved region were never fetched, the file's size nevertheless EQUALS the remote's, the meta stamps COMPLETE, and a stopped conversation serves the hole forever. Reproduced verbatim (dial transport, foreign 203-byte append during `fsReadRange`, remote past the cap): no error, no log line, `meta.size == remote size`, remote bytes [P, P+203) missing — and the next poll short-circuited on it. Three legs, each mutation-tested alone. **(i) The verdict is typed.** `slotMovedError` carries `code:'ESLOTMOVED'` and the catch re-throws it (`_isTerminalFetchError`): a statement about the CACHE is true on every rung; only TRANSPORT failures may degrade. **(ii) What a rung learned outlives it.** `observed.moved` (`_slotMovedEarlier`) denies the ssh rung a delta even when the slab rung reached the fallback for an unrelated reason — it can observe the movement, refetch whole UNDER the cap, and then have that whole read die on the wire. **(iii) The durable half.** One poll later the movement is invisible to `_appendDeltaAt`: the foreign bytes are simply in the file, the offset it re-reads MATCHES them, and the append lands right behind them — so the fix would only have delayed the corruption by one poll. `_prefixIsOurs(localSize, meta)` asks the meta instead of the clock: a delta may extend only bytes this cache's own writers stamped (the file may not hold MORE than `meta.size`). A SHORTER file stays growable on purpose — a positional append only ever writes at the end, so a short file is still a genuine prefix (a crash between the append and the meta write, a truncation), and refusing it would strand every over-cap slot that ever crashed mid-write. Over the cap every one of these is a loud failure that leaves cache AND meta byte-identical, and the cap error names WHICH fault it is ("could not be verified" vs "holds N bytes where the last fetch stamped M"). Two consequences fell out: the whole `cat` now stamps `buf.length` instead of the stat it was promised (a live transcript hands back more bytes than the probe said — the tail branch of the same rung has always stamped the real length, and an under-claiming meta would make (iii) read our own fetch as foreign bytes), and the data-plane catch now LOGS every fault it swallows VERBATIM — it logged nothing at all, which is how a degrade path hides a lost binding for three days (2.340.2).

**② e ROUND 6 — THE SHAPE ROUND 5 CONDEMNED BY MISTAKE (src/hosts.js).** Round 5's "grow only what we stamped" is right about foreign appends and wrong about one INNOCENT shape it cannot tell apart from them. Until round 5 the whole `cat` rung stamped the PROBE's stat while `cat` returned everything the live transcript had grown to since, so a perfectly healthy fetch left `meta.size` SHORTER than the file it wrote. Under the cap that costs one whole re-pull and heals; OVER the cap there is no whole re-pull, so `_prefixIsOurs` turned every such slot — a remote stopped thread that opened yesterday — into a permanent hard failure ("holds N bytes where the last fetch stamped M"), forever, because nothing on either side can ever move again. The heal cannot be a local scan: the corruption round 5 exists for appends ORDINARY TEXT (⑥g/E2), which no NUL/magic/record-boundary check can distinguish from our own missing bytes. So the only witness that can tell them apart is asked — THE REMOTE. When a slot is over the cap AND its meta predates the `sizeExact` marker AND the file is longer than that meta claims, the gap `[stamp, file)` is read back (a BOUNDED read of just the gap: `fsReadRange` on the slab rung, `tail -c +N | head -c LEN` on the ssh one) and compared byte-for-byte with what the cache holds there. Equal ⇒ genuine prefix, the stamp was merely short ⇒ re-stamp `meta.size = file size` with the marker, ONCE, and the delta rides again. Not equal ⇒ round 5's refusal stands, unchanged. Four bounds keep it from becoming a way around round 5 or a per-poll cost: only over the cap (under it a whole refetch is the remedy — adopting bytes you can simply re-fetch is trusting what you did not have to trust), only for marker-less metas (a round-5+ writer stamps what the file holds, so a longer file THERE is genuinely foreign), never on a slot a rung watched move in this window (`_slotMovedEarlier` — we already know where those bytes came from), and free local evidence FIRST (the round-3 whole-file splice scan, skipped when this poll's `cacheUsable` already paid for it). The verification is written to disk the moment it is PROVEN, not when the fetch that triggered it finishes — otherwise a delta that dies on the wire throws the proof away and the next poll pays again, on exactly the slots where the fetch is least likely to complete. A refusal, by contrast, is memoized IN MEMORY only (per slot, keyed by the size it was made on, 10 min): stamping a slot we would not serve freezes the corruption in, and the memo also carries the VERDICT so the replayed refusal gives the same diagnosis. Four verdicts, four different sentences appended to the cap error — a checked byte mismatch, a splice marker found locally, a slot seen moving, and "the remote could not be read (retryable)" — because a refusal that claims the check it skipped is "an error string is not a diagnosis" with the sign flipped. Second half, the round-5 MINOR's own cost: the degrade line round 5 added is now rate-limited per HOST (≤1/60s, `_warnDegradeOnce`, carrying `(+N similar suppressed…)` onto the next window) — the thing it degrades on is usually persistent, and every attach/poll/goal-sync tick comes through there, so a line per fetch is a journal that says one sentence.

**② f ROUND 7 — THE HEAL'S OWN COSTS, AND A GUARD THAT NEVER ASKED WHAT IT WAS COMPARING (src/hosts.js).** The round-6 verify named three, each reproduced before it was fixed. **(i) "Paid once per slot" was paid twice per poll, every poll.** The whole-file splice scan is a SYNCHRONOUS read of the entire cache file on the event loop, and round 6's own comment claimed it was "skipped when this poll's `cacheUsable` already paid for it" — true only within ONE rung. `cacheUsable` runs on the slab rung, the slab rung's "too large" is degradable, so the ssh rung runs it again: two whole reads of a 64 MB slot per poll. And "per poll" is the worse half — the slots that owe the scan are exactly the ones that REFUSE, and a refused poll leaves the meta byte-identical BY DESIGN (round 6's disk invariant), so nothing ever retires the legacy marker: every session poll, window attach and goal-sync tick re-read the whole file, forever. The verdict is a statement about BYTES, so it is now memoized against the bytes it was made on — the cache file's identity (inode, size, mtime), 10 min TTL, dropped explicitly by every writer in the fetch that touches the slot. That makes it free across rungs AND across polls, so the `deepDone` flag is gone: the heal asks the question outright instead of inferring from a flag whether some earlier caller happened to have answered it. The scan itself now answers in THREE states — clean / a marker is in there / the file could not be read — because only a VERDICT may be remembered: the caller refuses the poll either way, but memoizing an unreadable file would let a transient EMFILE freeze a healthy slot for the whole TTL (the same line round 6 drew between a refusal and 'unverified'). Invariant: **a "paid once" claim is about a CACHE, and a cache needs a key that names what it is about — the flag that says "someone already did this" is only true inside the scope that set it.** **(ii) A rate limiter that suppressed the wrong thing.** Round 6's `_warnDegradeOnce` keyed the 60 s window on the HOST id alone, so the first fault held the whole minute and a DIFFERENT fault on that host inside it — the heal's gap probe dying while the link flaps, a free identifier an extraction left behind (the 2.340.2 class this line exists to expose) — was dropped into a "+N similar" tally that names nothing: the rate limiter became the silent catch it replaced. "Similar" has to MEAN similar, so the window is keyed on the host AND the message's CLASS (`_degradeClassKey`: the same sentence with its offsets/sizes/ids blanked). Each distinct fault speaks once a minute; only genuine repeats are counted. **(iii) The gap comparison never asked whether it was still the file it measured.** The heal compares the cache's `[stamp, EOF)` with the remote's own bytes, and the two sides are taken at DIFFERENT times: `localSize` from a stat before the remote round trip, the local bytes after it. A slot that shrinks in between (a truncation, a non-atomic writer, an operator repairing it) hands back a short region, and the pre-fix code read that mismatch as the strongest verdict it has — 'foreign' ("the bytes past that point were CHECKED against the remote: they are not its own") — and MEMOIZED it for ten minutes, so one transient shrink condemned a perfectly healthy over-cap slot and the memo replayed the mis-diagnosis on every later poll with no probe left to correct it. `_healGapIsStillOurs` (neuterable, like every other leg here) re-stats before comparing: a slot that changed under the probe is the MOVED class — terminal for this poll, remembered by nobody, re-judged next poll. Invariant: **a comparison between two reads taken at different times must prove they are about the same object before it may report the DIFFERENCE as evidence — and a fact about a race may never be memoized as a fact about the bytes.** Pins: test-codex-zst (M)/(N)/(O) — the pre-fix scan count (3, then +2 per poll) against one scan for the slot over three polls and two rungs, plus memo-cleared and bytes-changed negative controls; both key shapes A/B'd on the same two faults; the shrink race reproduced as a memoized 'foreign' that survives the file's recovery, then as a shifted verdict whose very next poll heals the slot.

**③ A transcript shorter than the magic could never be verified (src/hosts.js).** `head.length < 4 ⇒ refetch` meant an empty or one-record transcript was fully re-pulled on EVERY poll, forever. A cache under 4 bytes cannot BE a zstd frame and cannot hide a splice: it verifies (still subject to "a .jsonl cache starts with a record"), while a sub-magic cache under a COMPRESSED remote is still refused.

**④ A home-dir dot glob widened to every user (src/plugin-manifest.js).** 2.369.44 taught the validator that node reads a declared fs path only up to the first `*` and grants by STRING PREFIX; 2.369.43 taught it to collapse `.` segments the way node does. At `~/.*` the two rules collide: node keeps `.*` as an ordinary segment (the grant is the prefix `<home>/.` — dotfiles), but collapsing the dot HERE emitted `<home>*` — the whole home directory plus every sibling path starting with those characters (`/home/u*` reaches `/home/u2/…`). The manifest said one thing and node was handed another, invisibly. A `.` segment immediately before `*` is now refused, naming the prefix it would have produced.

**⑤–⑦ The ACP bookkeeping nudge is OURS, not the user's (data/bin/acp-wrapper.js).** Three consequences of one confusion:
  ⑤ The 'queue-cleared' notice ("send it again") subtracted dropped NUDGE entries but not dropped PEER ones — and a peer message is not the user's to re-send either: it goes back to the delivery ladder on `peer_result ok:false` and arrives again on its own, so the advice was both wrong and impossible to follow. Nudges AND peers are subtracted now; the log line keeps the true total.
  ⑥ The nudge queued with a visible `queued_input`, painting a "Queued" chip on a bubble that does not exist. It queues with `silentQueue` (as peer messages already did) and stays visible in `queue_changed` as kind 'system', so the strip is still honest about what is waiting.
  ⑦ **A Stop landing inside the `/api/agent/stop-check` round trip still billed a turn.** The nudge is fetched by `endPrompt` and dispatched when the answer lands — by then the interrupt has finished and `activePrompt` is null, so the fetch resolved straight into `runPrompt` and started a fresh turn the user had just asked to stop; the only trace was a reminder appearing out of nowhere. The interrupt now bumps `stopEpoch` and aborts the request in flight, and an answer whose epoch is gone is discarded with a logged reason. The latch is never set on that path, so the NEXT completed turn nudges normally.

**INVARIANTS.** (a) A verification window sized for the moment of corruption expires the moment anything is appended after it — a decision that ADOPTS unverified bytes must pay for evidence over the whole artifact, and record that it did so, so the cost is paid once. (a2) That record must be a marker THIS code writes (a schema version), never a field the buggy code also wrote: gating a heal on "the old code left this blank" misses every slot the old code filled in on its way past the bug — and a fix's own release window is where those slots come from. (a3) Name the harm the fix removes, then prove the fix removes THAT: a change that is separately correct ("record the adoption") can be shipped with a false causal claim attached ("…so growth syncs"), and a test that quietly relaxes the condition under test (dropping `maxBytes` on the growth poll) will agree with it. (a4) Advice inside an error message is a claim about the system — if the branch that emits it can only be reached where the advice cannot work, it is a false claim, not a courtesy. (a5) A guard nobody can delete-and-fail is not a guard: every legality clause owes a leg that goes RED when that clause alone is removed — and when the clause exists in twin form on two rungs, it owes one on EACH, or the copies drift the moment one is edited. (a6) A fast path added under an existing one must leave the slow path REACHABLE: replacing the only road with an optimisation turns every failure mode of the optimisation into a failure of the whole feature, and on the LAST rung there is nothing below to catch it — fall back where the slow path is still possible, and hard-fail only where it provably is not. (a7) A meta writer overwrites what it just LEARNED, never what it inherited: durable provenance and a fresh object literal are contradictory designs, and the field that gets erased is always the one that cannot be re-earned. (a8) A read-then-write against a size measured before the read is a lost update waiting for a second caller: serialize the RESOURCE (the cache slot), and make the write itself refuse the offset it was computed from once that offset is no longer the end of the file — a positional write at a re-checked offset is idempotent where a blind append is a duplicated region. Corollary for the repro: a duplication whose records are IDENTICAL is byte-identical to the correct file, so a fixture built from repeated records will pass the negative control while the bug is fully present. (a9) **A refusal is only a refusal on the rung that can be reached.** A guard thrown inside a try whose catch degrades to another implementation is not a guard — it is a slower path to the same corruption, because the fallback re-derives its inputs from the very state the guard refused to trust. Classify the throw: a TRANSPORT failure may degrade, a verdict about the shared RESOURCE must be terminal on every rung and must travel as a CODE the fallback tests, never as a message nobody reads. (a10) **A guard that only sees the race window has to be paired with one that reads the evidence the race left behind.** An in-window check (re-read the offset through the fd you are about to write with) expires the moment the poll ends: next time round, the foreign bytes are simply part of the file and every in-window question answers "fine". The durable form asks a record the code wrote itself — grow only what we stamped — which also means every writer must stamp what the file ACTUALLY holds, or the invariant starts reading our own fetches as foreign. Corollary: when a fix's negative control needs three separate neuterings to reproduce the original bug, that is not redundancy to trim — it is three independent legs, and the suite should be able to remove each one alone and go red. (a11) **A rule written for a corruption will also condemn every innocent shape that wears the same signature — find them before shipping it, because the rule's own remedy may not exist for them.** "The file holds more than we stamped" is foreign bytes AND our own short stamp, and the branch where a whole refetch is impossible is exactly where the innocent one dies permanently. Two consequences: gate the strict rule on a marker THIS code writes so it is absolute only where it is provably right (a10's corollary — a writer that stamps the real length cannot over-stamp), and when local evidence cannot separate the two shapes, ASK THE SOURCE OF TRUTH for the disputed bytes instead of loosening the rule (a bounded read that compares them is positive evidence; a splice scan is only absence of one kind of evidence). The heal that follows must be bounded to the case with no other remedy, paid once, durable the moment it is proven, and its refusals must be remembered in MEMORY — a refusal stamped on disk freezes the corruption in. (a12) A degrade-path log obeys the "say what you swallowed" law AND the "do not say it 500 times" one: rate-limit per host and carry the suppressed count forward — a suppressed fault is not a forgotten fault. (b) Any rewrite a validator performs on a declared path must be a rewrite the consumer performs too; where they differ, refuse rather than emit the wider form (a normalization that widens a grant is a silent privilege escalation). (c) The rule "count only what the USER did" has to enumerate everything that is not the user — one exception (our nudge) is a hint that there are others (a peer message). (d) An in-flight request belongs to the turn that started it: when that turn is cancelled, record the cancellation and DISCARD the answer — never let a round trip that outlived its turn start a billed one.

**Pins.** scripts/test-codex-zst.mjs ⑥c (a buried splice; a magic straddling the 1 MiB chunk boundary; a hybrid that ALREADY carries provenance — refused, because `sameRemote` is not evidence; the one-time cost proven by counting the scan's own reads across two polls; a >1 MiB clean over-cap slot that must still adopt; the ssh rung's stamp AND its delta growth under the cap it was adopted with, plus the live-overtake, short-read and unverifiable-cache negative controls; the honest cap error with a "do exactly what the old text said" control; tiny-transcript short-circuit with two negative controls — every one red with its own fix reverted); ⑥d/⑥e (round 3: a re-compressed .zst remote over a .zst cache refetched WHOLE on BOTH rungs, with the guard-less legality predicate asserted to call that growth legal and the spliced bytes shown to decompress to the OLD transcript — both legs red when the compression clause is deleted from their own rung; a `tail` the host cannot run and a short tail both falling back to the whole cat under the cap, with an over-cap negative control proving the fallback is not a cap bypass; `adopted`/`slab` surviving a delta on both rungs and cleared by a whole refetch); ⑥f/⑥g (rounds 4-5: the two overlapping fetches and the moved offset; then the verifier's dial-transport scenario — slab rung first, ssh rung reachable, remote past the cap, a foreign 203-byte append during `fsReadRange` — refused on BOTH rungs with cache+meta byte-identical, refused AGAIN on the next poll by the stamped-size leg alone, each leg isolated (only the typed verdict removed still refuses, but the caller loses the diagnosis), the pre-fix shape restored by neutering all three legs and shown to seal the corrupt slot and serve it forever, the degrade line asserted verbatim with a healthy-fetch control, and the overtaking whole `cat` stamping what it fetched with a pre-fix-meta control); ⑥h (round 6: an over-cap slot seeded with the old stat-sized meta and a CLEAN longer cache — the pre-fix permanent failure reproduced first (refused twice, nothing can ever move), then healed by one bounded probe of exactly [stamp, file) and grown by the ordinary delta, on the ssh rung AND its slab twin; the heal proven durable across a delta that dies on the wire; a DIRTY longer cache of the same length and with no splice marker refused with the checked-mismatch diagnosis, cache+meta byte-identical, its verdict memoized in memory and re-probed once the memo is cleared; a buried splice marker refused with NO remote read and without claiming the check it skipped; the same shape under the cap refetched whole with no probe at all; a marker-carrying meta refused without asking anyone; a probe that dies on the wire refusing as RETRYABLE and healing on the next poll; and the degrade line rate-limited per host with a second host and a window-expiry leg — ten mutations, each reddening the suite alone); scripts/test-plugin-security.mjs (`~/.*` / `/srv/a/.*` refused, the pre-fix outputs `/home/u*` / `/srv/a*` asserted never produced, `~/.config/*` and `~/./*` unaffected); scripts/test-acp-harness.mjs (the peer entry excluded from the notice, the nudge's silent queue entry, and a Stop held inside a deliberately delayed stop-check with a same-server control that proves the delay itself still nudges).
## THE WRITER SWEEP KILLED READERS, AND ITS TWIN NEVER RAN AT ALL (B-3185, src/writer-sweep.js + src/cli-identity.js + src/server/boot-restore.js + src/hosts.js + src/routes/sessions.js)

Two defects in the same scan, one of them destructive and one of them silently dead.

**(1) A substring decided who dies.** The pre-resume sweep finds processes holding a conversation's transcript open and SIGTERMs the ones that are the agent CLI. "Is it the CLI" was `case "$(ps -p $pid -o args=)" in *claude*)` — a substring over the WHOLE command line — so ANY process whose argv merely NAMED a path under `~/.claude` qualified: `tail -f ~/.claude/projects/<id>.jsonl`, an editor with the transcript open, and a test or agent process running from a git worktree under `~/.claude/worktrees/`, which is where the worktree-only-smokes law puts every agent. The suite hit this first: holding the transcript fd in its own process, it matched its own guard and SIGTERMed itself — exit 143, `npm run ci` red, on code that was fine — and the response at the time was a WORKAROUND IN THE TEST (spawn a holder with a neutral argv) rather than a fix, so the live hazard stayed. Same bug in the codex legs via `*codex*`. Same-origin A/B, identical fixtures, only the script swapped: OLD kills `tail -f` and the worktree process (both `survived=false`); NEW leaves both alive and still kills the real writer.

FIX: `vs_is_cli <pid> <claude|codex>` decides by the EXECUTABLE, in the three rungs that cover the three shapes the CLIs ship in — argv[0]'s basename (native install / vendor binary), the interpreter's FIRST NON-FLAG operand when argv[0] is node/bun/deno (npm shebang shim, `…/claude-code/cli.js`, `…/codex/bin/codex.js`), and `/proc/<pid>/exe` (a native claude execs `~/.local/share/claude/versions/<ver>`). argv comes NUL-split from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` where it exists, `ps -o args=` elsewhere. A later ARGUMENT never identifies a process. The fd evidence stays the PRIMARY signal — vs_is_cli only answers writer-or-reader — and the codex ARGV leg (`codex resume <tid>` / `CODEX_WEBUI_RESUME_ID=<tid>`) is deliberately exempt because it is self-evidencing and the shape it catches (an orphaned wrapper / its dtach master) is not the codex binary.

**(2) The scan forked per process, and its twin forked per FD.** Measured on the dev box (32 cores, loadavg 3.4–4.8, 3678–3713 processes, 405,735 open fds): the sweep's `ls`+`grep` PAIR per process = 7356 forks and 9.9–12.2 s wall against a 20 s budget — over half of it spent on fork/exec, and past the budget entirely on a busier or bigger machine. boot-restore's "which conversations does a live claude still hold?" probe was the same scan written a second time as a `readlink` fork PER FD — ~6.4 minutes here — against a 6 s timeout, so it ALWAYS timed out, and a bare `catch {}` turned that into "nobody is live": the boot socket dedup silently lost its liveness signal and fell back to createdAt. (Its `/proc/[0-9]*/fd/*` glob was NOT an ARG_MAX case, which is what the first fix's comment claimed and the r2 review corrected: a shell `for` loop expands in the shell's own memory and never reaches execve. ARG_MAX bounds `vs_fd_chunk`, which EXECS `ls` — that is why THAT is chunked.) FIX: ONE shared `fdScanShellFns()` in writer-sweep.js — one `ls -l` per 400 fd directories + one awk, ~20 forks, 3.0–3.5 s — embedded verbatim by both backend scripts and by boot-restore, whose failures now LOG. A/B on the same fixture: old probe `FAILED after 6007ms ETIMEDOUT`, new probe `ok in 3160ms` with the holder named.

**WHAT THE FIELD MEASUREMENT ALSO SAID (do not overclaim this fix).** The installed claude (2.1.226, native) does NOT hold its transcript open — it appends and closes; a machine-wide scan found ZERO holders of any `~/.claude/projects/**.jsonl` with 16 CLIs running. So (i) the claude FD leg mostly finds READERS, not writers — which is exactly why a substring identity test there was dangerous rather than merely sloppy — and the leg that actually reaches a live claude is its own `~/.claude/sessions/<pid>.json` lock file (unchanged, now behind the same executable test); (ii) boot-restore's probe is fixed as a MECHANISM (no stall, no silent catch) but still answers "nobody" for current claude, so `claudeAlive` remains effectively false locally. Giving it the CLI registry keyed with `procStart` is the real fix and is deliberately NOT in this commit: retiring is destructive (SIGTERM the owner + unlink the dtach socket), and changing which socket boot destroys is its own decision, not a rider on a performance fix.
