**Fix — the check belongs at the ACQUISITION point.** `adopt()` returns null when `state.stopping`: one guard over every rung, present and future, because it is the only place a client is published. `locate()` additionally re-checks after the isolated-cwd await, after the reuse health probe (so a disable never even evaluates the reuse verdict, nor signals a recorded pid on behalf of a service already being torn down) and after `freePort()` (never START a daemon for a service that was turned off mid-ladder). The boot loop bails through `abandon()`, which SIGTERMs the child and clears the record only when it names that port; its `why` is set only when WE decided, so a child that crashed on its own keeps `onChildExit`'s honest `lastError`.

**Invariants**: ① round 6's rule generalises — the guard goes where OWNERSHIP is taken, and for a keeper that is the single publisher (`adopt`), not the entry of the function that walks toward it; ② a state a client reads as "we are running" (`ready`) must never be published by a code path that started before the user said stop — `stopped:true` and `ready:true` cannot both be answers to the same question; ③ a stop that is only correct because some OTHER fact (a plugin record written first) happened to be flipped first is not a stop — make the primitive itself authoritative; ④ never walk away from a process you spawned: a bail path that leaves the child AND its record turns "off" into "adopted again on the next boot"; ⑤ the negative control is the whole proof — every leg here runs twice, once against `src/opencode-serve.js` and once against a copy with exactly these 6 checks removed, and that copy must publish `ready`, arm a lane, leak an inotify wd and fire; its replacements are asserted to match their expected counts so a stale control fails loudly instead of turning the A/B into two green arms.


## A LEVEL IS NOT A CANCELLATION: A DISABLE→ENABLE PAIR WAS SERVED BY THE LADDER THE DISABLE KILLED — S9-remainder round 8 (2026-09-07, B-eac2)

Round 7 moved the guard to the ACQUISITION point (`adopt`, the only place `state.client` is ever assigned) and re-checked after each await that `stop()` cannot cancel. It guarded on `state.stopping` — and that is a **LEVEL, not a cancellation**: `stop()` raises it and the very next `start()` lowers it again. So the guard is not terminal across a **Disable→Enable pair** — the panel's own Stop/Start, or a user who turns it off and changes their mind. `start()` clears `state.stopping` while `ensure()` still holds the in-flight `locate()`, so the Enable does not begin a ladder: it JOINS the cancelled one, which then sails past every re-check and publishes evidence it gathered **before** the stop.

**Measured, unfixed, through the real wiring** (`install()` → `locator.start()` → `locator.stop({killRecorded:true})` → `locator.start()` — the `_ocStop`/`_ocStart` pair in src/plugins.js — against a RECORDED serve that outlived a restart and answers in 700 ms, with a real `createLiveLane` over a real store dir): Disable at T, Enable at T+50 ms, both inside the reuse `/global/health` probe ⇒ **two** adoptions. The Enable's own ladder spawned and adopted a serve, and then the stale ladder woke up and adopted `source:'reused'` **on the exact port `stop({killRecorded})` had just SIGTERMed and whose record it had deleted** — overwriting the live one. The published client pointed at a dead process; the spawned child it displaced was still running with nothing tracking it; `state.child` had been destroyed before either. And with the epoch alone, the mirror failure: the Enable's `start()` resolves the CANCELLED attempt's `null`, so the route reports a click that did nothing.

**Fix — per-attempt cancellation, the shape round 6 used for the lane.** `state.stopEpoch` is bumped by `stop()` **and** by every new `locate()`; `locate()` captures `const epoch = ++state.stopEpoch` at its top and passes it into `adopt(port, pid, source, epoch)` and every post-await check, all of which now ask `cancelled(epoch) = state.stopping || epoch !== state.stopEpoch`. A cancelled attempt can never become valid again, and only the NEWEST attempt may take ownership. `stop()` also DETACHES the in-flight attempt (`ensuring = null`) so the Enable's `ensure()` starts its OWN ladder, and `ensure()`'s settle handler only clears the slot it still owns (`if (ensuring === p)`) — otherwise a late-finishing cancelled ladder drops the live one that replaced it. `abandon()`'s `why` is now set only while `state.stopping` holds: a run cancelled by a NEWER ladder must not write a complaint that ladder is about to contradict.

**Invariants**: ① a flag that the next user action clears is a LEVEL and cannot cancel work already in flight — cancellation needs a token the in-flight work captured (an epoch), and "newer attempt wins" must be part of it, not just "a stop happened"; ② the retry after a cancel must be a NEW attempt: a single-flight cache that hands the next caller the promise of the run just cancelled turns Enable into a silent no-op, so the canceller detaches the slot and the settle handler only clears the slot it still owns; ③ a bail path may only write `lastError` while its own stop is still the current story — a cancelled run that narrates over the run that replaced it publishes a stale complaint on a healthy service; ④ `abandon()` SIGTERMs the child it holds in its CLOSURE, never the one in `state.child` (`stop()` nulled that first, and after an Enable that slot belongs to a newer ladder) — the conditional null is a no-op on every path today and stays conditional for exactly that reason, pinned by a leg whose mock child records its kills by phase; ⑤ the negative controls are one per mechanism, 2 of them, each neutering exactly ONE check so the two failures stay distinguishable (drop the epoch ⇒ two adoptions, the last one a SIGTERMed process; drop the detach ⇒ the Enable's own `start()` resolves null), plus a third that removes `abandon()`'s SIGTERM so the kill assert can fail. Gate: scripts/test-opencode-s9.mjs ROUND 8.


## A RECORD YOU DO NOT OWN: A CANCELLED LADDER DELETED THE LIVE ONE'S RECORD AND ORPHANED THE SERVE — S9-remainder round 9 (2026-09-07, B-eac2)

Round 8's `ensuring = null` is what makes an Enable start its OWN ladder — and it is also the first time **two ladders can be alive at once**. Every post-await site in `locate()` got the epoch check except the two in the **reuse rung**: `const bad = await unsafeReuseReason(probe, rec)` is an await, and its bad branch then ran `killPid(rec.pid,'SIGTERM'); clearRecord();` with no check at all, while `else if (!pidAlive(rec.pid)) clearRecord();` ran after a `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` health probe with none either. `abandon()` (the boot-loop bail) even states the rule the reuse rung was breaking: *"never clear a record that names a DIFFERENT serve"*.

**Measured, unfixed, through the real wiring** (`install()` → `locator.start()` → `locator.stop({killRecorded:true})` → `locator.start()` — the `_ocStop`/`_ocStart` pair in src/plugins.js — with a recorded serve that answers in 700 ms): Disable at T+1000 ms lands inside the VERDICT probe (past round 7's post-health check), Enable at T+1050 ms starts its own ladder, spawns a serve and writes its record; the stale ladder wakes at ~T+1400 ms, decides the serve it probed must be replaced, and **deletes the record the live ladder had just written**. Result: `ready:true`, `source:'spawned'`, a running `opencode serve` on a port nothing on disk names — the next boot can neither adopt it nor kill it, which is the orphaned-third-party-daemon class the record exists to prevent. Same outcome through the dead-pid arm (a recorded serve that never answers + a pid that is not running). The stale ladder also fired a SIGTERM of its own on behalf of a service the newer ladder now owns.

**Fix — every record write and clear is OWNED, and every post-await action in the reuse rung is behind `cancelled(epoch)`.** `clearRecord(owns)` takes the `{port, pid}` pair it believes is recorded and REFUSES when the record on disk names a different serve; `abandon()`, `onChildExit()` (the port travels from the spawn site — `state.port` is still null while a child dies during its boot wait) and `parkRunaway()` all name theirs. Port alone is not identity: `freePort()` hands out a port that is free RIGHT NOW, so the port an abandoned child is giving up is re-bindable by the very ladder that cancelled it, and abandon's old `r.port === port` test would then have deleted the NEW ladder's record — the pid discriminates. `stop()` keeps its UNCONDITIONAL clear deliberately: it is synchronous, it bumps the epoch first, and "the user turned the service off" outranks anything on disk.

**Invariants**: ① a record naming a live process is a promise to the NEXT boot — deleting one you do not own is how "off" becomes "a daemon nobody can find", so a clear must NAME what it believes is there and refuse a mismatch; ② once cancellation exists, "the file on disk is mine" stops being true by construction — every post-await ACTION (kill, clear, publish), not just the publish, needs the token; ③ port equality is not identity for anything that can be recycled, and the pair the writer wrote is the claim; ④ the safe unconditional clears are exactly the synchronous authority points that bump the epoch first, and they say so in a comment; ⑤ the fix is deliberately TWO layers, so the control that reproduces the original loss is a PRE-FIX copy removing all 3 replacements, while one copy per mechanism proves each layer is load-bearing alone (drop the verdict check ⇒ the cancelled ladder still SIGTERMs, two kills, and only the ownership layer saves the record; drop the ownership test ⇒ abandon() deletes a recycled-port record naming the live serve) — plus a positive control that a genuinely dead recorded pid is STILL cleared by an uncancelled ladder, because a guard that refuses every clear would be the same bug wearing the other hat. Gate: scripts/test-opencode-s9.mjs ROUND 9.

## THE RECORD WE WERE ABOUT TO OVERWRITE: A RECORDED SERVE THAT WAS ALIVE AND SILENT BECAME AN ORPHAN ON EVERY RESTART — S9-remainder round 10 (2026-09-07, B-eac2)

Rounds 7-9 hardened the reuse rung against a **concurrent** ladder. This one needs no concurrency at all, and it is older than all of them: the rung owned exactly ONE arm of an unanswered `/global/health` — a DEAD recorded pid (`} else if (!cancelled(epoch) && !pidAlive(rec.pid)) clearRecord(owned);`). An ALIVE one was neither killed, nor cleared, nor logged; control fell THROUGH to the spawn twenty lines below, whose `writeRecord` overwrote data/opencode-serve.json with the new child.

**Measured, unfixed, through the real wiring** (`install()` + `locator.start()`, a socket that accepts and never answers at the recorded port, a REAL live process at the recorded pid): `ready:true`, `source:'spawned'`, the record naming the new port — and the recorded process still running, with nothing on disk naming it. The runaway guard cannot see it (it samples OUR child's /proc), `stop({killRecorded})` cannot reach it (it reads the record), and the next boot cannot adopt it. Meanwhile OpenCode keeps indexing and inotify-watching whatever instance it had booted: the 2.369.42 resource shape, arriving through a different door, once per restart of a wedged serve, in silence.

**Fix — "we are about to overwrite this record" is an OWNED DECISION** (`settleRecordedServe`), with outcomes that are named rather than implied: `'clear'` (the recorded serve is provably gone), `'keep'` (we are not going to spawn, so nothing overwrites it and nothing may act on it — it still NAMES the process `stop({killRecorded})` would signal), `'answered'` (a longer confirm probe reached it after all ⇒ adopt it), `'blocked'`, `'cancelled'` (round 9's rule: a superseded ladder touches nothing). What a live pid IS gets its own PURE verdict, `classifyRecordedPid`, over injected procfs reads: `'ours'` = running `serve` with the RECORDED `--port` under our own uid (the port is the discriminator, not argv[0] — the `opencode` launcher may re-exec its runtime); `'other'` = a different uid, THIS server process, or a different command line, i.e. the serve is gone and the record is stale bookkeeping we may delete but whose pid we must never signal; `'unknown'` = procfs said nothing. Only `'ours'` may be stopped, and only after ONE longer confirm probe (`RECORD_CONFIRM_TIMEOUT_MS`, 4s: a cold 1.18.29 needs ~1.2s and a busy one more — **slow is not wedged**, and if it answers we adopt it and stop nothing), a warn naming pid/port/budget, and a BOUNDED wait for the exit. An unverifiable live pid, or one that ignores the SIGTERM, is `'blocked'`: no kill, no spawn, and a PARK whose `lastError` names the pid, the port, the record file and both ways out — parking because `storeFailureReason` (the /api/home reason + the `harness-store-updated` push, 2026-09-07) speaks ONLY for a park, so a park is what makes the user see it instead of a silent orphan. It re-tries on its own cooldown (`BLOCKED_RETRY_MS`, through the generalized `state.retryAfter` gate that now also carries the runaway's), because the state can heal without anyone: the serve may answer again, or the process may exit.

**Invariants**: ① `pidAlive` answers *"something runs under that number"*, never *"that is my serve"* — pids are recycled and a record outlives its process, so the two things a live pid licenses (a SIGNAL, and being OVERWRITTEN) each need positive identification, and they need DIFFERENT ones: kill only what you proved is yours, overwrite only what you proved is gone; ② a fall-through is not a decision — the arm nobody wrote is the arm nobody tested, and here the untested arm was the destructive one (a rung that owns a record must enumerate its outcomes, including "leave it alone"); ③ a probe budget is a statement about US, not about the process — before acting irreversibly on "it did not answer", ask once more with a budget that fits the slowest healthy case; ④ when you cannot prove either fact, refuse BOTH actions and say so where the user already looks (the park channel), because a silent orphan is the one outcome nobody can debug; ⑤ a new park kind must answer "how does it end?" — `retryAfter` is now the single gate, and every surface that renders a park (⚙ card, store reason) needs its own row or it will describe the new state as the old one. Gate: scripts/test-opencode-s9.mjs ROUND 10 (a PRE-FIX control whose 2 replacements restore the fall-through, one leg per verdict against REAL processes and REAL procfs, and four positive controls — healthy / slow / service-off / dead-pid — because a guard that refuses every clear would be the same bug wearing the other hat).

## A GUARD THAT CAN NEVER ANSWER IS AN OUTAGE, AND THE ONE BUTTON A PARK LEAVES ENABLED — S9-remainder round 11 (2026-09-07, B-eac2)

Round 10 made "we are about to overwrite this record" a decision — and hung two of its five outcomes on a `/proc` read. **macOS is "full support" in the README and has no `/proc` at all.** There `readProcCmdline` answers `null` for EVERY pid, so `classifyRecordedPid` returns `'unknown'` for every live one, and the `'unknown'` arm PARKS: a stale record whose number has since been recycled onto an unrelated program takes the store dark forever, fires the red toast on every page load, and tells the user to go stop a process that has nothing to do with us. That is strictly worse than the pre-round-10 fall-through it replaced. A verdict that can never be reached is not a guard; a verdict that is ALWAYS reached is not a guard either.

Three fixes, each reproduced first (the no-`/proc` host simulated by stubbing the readers, the remote script driven against a fake record and a real silent socket):

**① A PORTABLE IDENTITY RUNG, and "this HOST cannot answer" as its own verdict.** `readProcCmdline`/`readProcUid` fall back to `ps -p <pid> -o uid=,args=` (execFile, 2s, briefly memoised) — procfs first, `ps` where there is none, the ladder `src/discovery-facts.js` already uses for `pidLooksClaude`, and a VALUE read, never an existence probe (that stays `pidAlive`'s `kill -0`). Then the distinction the fix turns on: `hostCanIdentify()` asks the INJECTED reader about our OWN pid, once. A reader that cannot describe the process it is running inside has no procfs and no usable `ps`, so a live pid there carries no information — verdict `'blind'`, and the caller behaves like the pre-round-10 path: clear the stale record, signal NOTHING, spawn. `'unknown'` keeps its meaning and its park: *the reader normally answers and about THIS pid it did not*.

**② THE SHIPPED SSH SCRIPT WAS THE UNFIXED TWIN.** `data/bin/vibespace-opencode-op`'s `locate()` still probed the recorded port and, on any non-answer, spawned a second serve and overwrote the record — on SOMEONE ELSE'S machine, where there is no keeper, no runaway guard, no park and no ⚙ card, so the orphan can never be reached again. The settlement is ported verbatim in behaviour (dead pid ⇒ clear+spawn · provably not that serve ⇒ clear, never signalled · verified ours + still silent after the longer confirm probe ⇒ SIGTERM + bounded wait · unidentifiable or still running ⇒ `fail()` with the pid, the port, the record path and both ways out, instead of spawning over it), and `scripts/test-opencode-remote.mjs` now drives BOTH classifiers over one table (16 rows, verdict-for-verdict) plus the three budgets, then runs the REAL script through each verdict against a real silent socket and real processes — with a PRE-FIX copy of the script as the negative control (it starts the second serve and orphans the first).

**③ `stop({killRecorded})` SIGTERMed THE VERY PID THE PARK HAD REFUSED TO TOUCH.** In a blocked park every OpenCode control is dark except Disable — and Disable was `killPid(state.pid || rec.pid)` behind nothing but a self-pid guard. It now routes through the same verdict (`decideRecordedKill`): the pid of a serve we are TALKING to (the client is the identity proof), or a recorded pid whose verdict is `'ours'`; anything else is left alone, the record is still cleared (the user's "off" outranks the file), and `lastError` SAYS what was not signalled and why.

**Invariants**: ① a guard added under incident pressure must be **reachable AND escapable on every platform the product claims** — "no evidence obtainable here" is a fact about the MACHINE and must be told apart from "no evidence about this pid", by probing the one pid whose answer we already know (our own); ② a fallback rung for a missing kernel interface is a VALUE read (`ps -o …=`), never an existence probe — liveness stays `kill -0`; ③ every control a park leaves enabled must obey the park's own verdict, or the park is only advice; ④ the CS-separation law applies to DECISIONS, not just to data walks: when the local settlement changes, the shipped single-file rung changes in the same commit with a parity assert that runs both classifiers over the same inputs (and a table row per decision boundary — an unpinned `serve`/`--port` half let a one-sided edit through in the first draft of that very assert); ⑤ a negative control whose deadline is 1 ms in the future is a coin toss, not a control — the cooldown a control depends on must actually elapse.


### VNC POINTER OFFSET UNDER DPI ZOOM (2.369.5, inc-mtdrm922 — userW + owner repro at DPI 90%)

Right-click on the in-container desktop highlighted the remote menu row ABOVE the cursor. Under the body `zoom: var(--ui-scale)`, noVNC mixes viewport px (clientX/gBCR) with layout px (clientWidth) → remote pointer ≈ actual × zoom, error growing with distance. **Invariant: the VNC canvas must live at NET zoom 1** — desktop-window's container carries `zoom: calc(1 / var(--ui-scale, 1))` (var-reactive, calc-in-zoom Chromium-verified). Any future embedded-canvas surface (xterm is internally consistent today) that mixes coordinate spaces gets the same counter-zoom treatment, never per-event math patches.

## RECONNECT RE-ATTACH REBUILD STORM (2.369.2, inc-mtd2pg6x "刚刚又卡死了")

A ws reconnect re-attaches every session; each attach rebuilt the window's whole DOM even when NOTHING changed — N windows × hundreds of messages in one task (5.5s stall co-timed with `state-resync` in the capture). **Invariant: loadHistory must no-op on an IDENTICAL slab** (same normEpoch + total + head/tail message ids + tail-anchored + not teleported) — meta/chatStatus/taskState/live state/typing indicator still apply, the DOM rebuild is skipped (`loadHistory:identical-skip` trace tag). Any future attach-path change must keep the skip reachable (it sits BEFORE the epoch write — writing the epoch first would defeat the comparison). Pins: test-chat-trim-guard.

## DIALOG EATEN BY SELECT-DRAG + DESKTOP-SWITCH PAGING STORM (2.369.1, inc-mtd1c2sd + inc-mtd1d0ft)

① The legacy shared dialog overlay (app.js `hideDialogs` wiring) closed on CLICK with `target === overlay`. A text-selection drag from inside an input releasing outside fires the click on the common ancestor (= the overlay) → the dialog vanished mid-form ("我选中个东西结果对话框没了"). **Invariant: backdrop dismissal must key on where the interaction STARTED (mousedown), never on the click target alone.** createModalShell already did; any new dialog shell must too.

② Desktop switches froze 30-60s (compositor stalls in the capture): visibility-flipping 4-6 chat windows re-measures all their content-visibility state; geometry transits through sh≈ch and every window's paging machinery reacted at once (fill-loops, extendTop storms, pin restores). **Invariant: a desktop-hidden ChatView is SUSPENDED — no paging/pin/boundary decisions off meaningless geometry** (`setSuspended`, wired in desktop-manager `_hideWin`/`_showWin` + stage-manager's direct `_hiddenByDesktop` writers; resume arms `_lastStructuralAt` so the existing settle guard covers the re-measure, pinned views re-tail in ONE hop; **2.369.9: re-tail means the LIVE tail — a behind-the-tail window (mobile touch paging trims the tail, windowEnd<total) takes the full jumpToBottom, else the old-position landing of inc-mtfi6034**). Any new code path that hides/shows windows wholesale must flow the suspend flag. **2.369.3 third leg (inc-mtd54h45): desktop-hidden CHAT windows also get `content-visibility: hidden` — bare visibility:hidden still styles/lays out the tree每帧 and DISCARDS the auto items' rendering state (the switch re-measure source); 'hidden' skips the subtree AND preserves the cache, so a switch repaints instead of re-measuring. Chat only — terminals carry WebGL canvases.** Pins: scripts/test-chat-trim-guard.mjs (14).

## FOLD-DOMINATED TRIM WHITE-SCREEN (2.368.29, inc-mtajy6wr — owner "上翻的时候出现大量白屏")

Semantic collapse (2.368.22-24) folds whole tool/agent runs; the c1206711-class transcripts (thousands of consecutive tool ops) render a 150-message window as a couple of run headers — SHORTER than the viewport, so scrollHeight clamps to clientHeight (captured 787=787 on every landing). `_trimBottom`'s fixed cap then removed the only VISIBLE content on each upward page and every wheel-tick teleported the window 50 messages deeper (ws 4572→4036 in ~6s) over blank space. The 2.301-2.307 collapsed-geometry guards all fired (`collapsedGeomSkip` on every landing) but they gate SCROLL decisions — the trim runs inside `_extendTop` regardless.

**Invariant: a rendered window shorter than ~2 viewports must GROW, not slide.** Both trims (`_trimBottom`/`_trimTop` — symmetric, downward paging through folds is the mirror image) raise the cap to 600 while `scrollHeight < clientHeight * 2`; 600 is the absolute DOM bound (fold members are hidden, cheap). Known residual: a single fold run longer than 600 messages re-enters the slide regime — acceptable until seen in the field. Pins: scripts/test-chat-trim-guard.mjs (6). The chatTraces ring (extendTop:done/trimBottom/collapsedGeomSkip tags) is the capture channel that caught this — keep it.

## Bug Fixes Applied
_Recent invariant-bearing entries only. The ancient one-liner chronicle (2026-03→07 era, ~150 fixes) moved verbatim to docs/history-archive.md — check there before re-diagnosing an old-sounding symptom._
- A CAPABILITY THAT ARRIVES AFTER THE PIXELS (2026-09-06, queue/steer round-3 review, caught before shipping): the `Queued` chip on a user bubble is built by `renderUserMsg`, which asks the view `_canSteerQueue()` and passes `onSteer = null` when the answer is no — and `applyQueueChip` renders a null-handler chip as `disabled`. But `_queueSupported` starts FALSE by construction (the running-wrapper gate of the round-2 fix), and BOTH of its sources land AFTER the bubbles: `loadHistory` renders every message and only then calls `_applyLiveMeta` with the attach payload's `queueSupported`, and a live session's baseline `queue_changed` arrives some frames after the first bubbles. So after ANY history load the chip rendered permanently dead — visible, wrong, and silent on click — while every grep-level pin and the round-2 functional assert (which set `_queueSupported: true` on the stub BEFORE rendering) stayed green. FIX: `_setQueueSupported` is the ONE writer of the flag; a change in EITHER direction schedules `_refreshQueueChips()` (rAF-coalesced, one pass over `_elements`, re-running `ChatRenderers.applyQueueChip` for every rendered message that carries a `queueState`). false→true makes the chips live, true→false makes them inert — a control that cannot work must not look like it can. INVARIANTS: ① when a capability decides how a widget RENDERS, the capability's setter owns re-rendering the widgets already on screen — a render-time-only read is correct exactly once, at a moment nobody controls; ② a functional test whose fixture starts in the FINAL state tests nothing about ordering — the assert must render BEFORE the capability arrives and then deliver it (both messengers: the attach payload and the wrapper's own meta op); ③ the strip escaped this bug only because it re-renders from `_setQueue` — sibling surfaces fed by the same flag must each be checked for who rebuilds them. Gate: test-queue-steer ⑦ (both orderings + the reverse flip + the one-writer pin), verified with a two-sided negative control (breaking either writer reddens only that path's asserts).
- THE CREATOR NEVER GETS AN 'attached' — LIVE STATE MUST RIDE 'created' (2.368.4, owner after updating for 2.368.3: "切换+resume还是停留在默认"; the style was verifiably ACTIVE — display only): a resume's creating client loads history over HTTP with NO meta and never receives an `attached` payload, so per-session live state (output style, auto-resume) that only rode the attach payload never reached the chip on the PRIMARY flow the feature exists for — pre-2.368.3 that surfaced as the eternally-pending hourglass (the live value never arrived, so the saved pick always looked pending), post-2.368.3 as a flat "默认". Second half: `attachSession`'s attached handler copied the payload into meta KEY BY KEY and the hand list lacked `outputStyle`/`autoResume`/`streamingKind` — the whitelist-drift class, FIFTH strike. FIX: `created` always carries `outputStyle`/`autoResume` (null = CLI default; the client's carries-the-key guard needs the key PRESENT), a shared `ChatView._applyLiveMeta` applies it everywhere, and both attach-shaped call sites pass the server payload WHOLESALE — the payload IS the meta, never a re-copied subset. INVARIANTS: ① adding a field to one reply payload ⇒ enumerate EVERY path a window is born through (create, attach, view, restart-reset) and prove each one delivers it — the creator/attacher split is invisible until a field only rides one of them; ② never hand-copy a payload into a meta object key by key (that list is a whitelist and whitelists drift — five strikes now); pinned in test-auto-resume (58).
- THE SERVER KNEW THE ADDRESS, THE UI STILL GUESSED IT (2.367.3, owner mapped an frp URL and every surface kept showing the LAN hostname): 2.367.0 gave the server ONE correct resolver for "this instance's URL", but four client surfaces each glued a path onto `location.origin` (design popover, publish dialog, chat `/p/<id>` linkify, Ports path mount) and the design row even preferred the relative `path` over the server's already-absolute `url`. Resolving centrally on the server is worthless while every consumer re-derives the same value locally. FIX: `utils.absUrl()` is the single joiner (instance public address → browser origin), the address rides `/api/home` at boot AND the `instance-url` broadcast live, and each surface lost its own join — pinned NEGATIVELY per file. INVARIANT: when a value gets an authoritative resolver, GREP FOR ITS RE-DERIVATIONS in the other tier and route them through it — a "single source of truth" that only one tier consults is two sources of truth (same class as the standing twin-set sweep).
- SILENT DROP MADE A QUIET TRUTH CHANNEL UNDIAGNOSABLE (2.367.2, the 2.367.1 follow-up): with arrival counters in place the CI failure named itself in one run — `posts=1 kept=2 events={api_request:2}` — so the CLI exported, the parser understood, and the records died at `if (!rec.rid || !rec.orgUuid) continue`: the CI identity (a personal setup-token) emits `api_request` with NO `organization.id`. The drop was correct; being SILENT was not — in production the same line turns a working exporter into an empty stash with no clue. Ingest now counts `noRid`/`noOrg`/`stashed`, and the E2E asserts the reachable truth (env→exporter→receiver→parser) instead of an account property. INVARIANT: a `continue` that discards otherwise-valid input must COUNT its reason — the difference between "nothing arrived" and "we dropped it, here is why" is the whole diagnosis, and one counter turned a 20-push mystery into a one-run answer.
- CI WAS RED FOR 20+ PUSHES AND ONLY THE OWNER SAW IT (2.367.1, owner: "我怎么老收到ci失败的邮件提醒?"): from 2.361.0 (the release that ADDED it) to 2.367.0, every GitHub Actions push failed on one assertion — the chat E2E's OTel truth check — while the local pre-push gate was green every time. Two separate faults. ① The assertion demanded a property of the ENVIRONMENT (does this runner's CLI export OpenTelemetry logs) as if it were a property of our code, and could not distinguish "the CLI exported nothing here" from "our parser dropped what it sent", so it failed instead of skipping. ② Nobody looked: `npm run ci` passing locally says nothing about the Actions mirror, and the only feedback channel was an email to the OWNER — 20+ of them. FIX: the receiver counts arrivals (posts/rejected/kept/seen) and exposes `GET /api/otel-stats`; the E2E SKIPs loudly with those counters when zero posts arrived and still hard-fails when posts arrived without a usable api_request. INVARIANTS: ① an assertion about a capability the environment may not have must classify the miss (skip with evidence) — never fail blind; ② after a push, CHECK the mirror (`gh run list --limit 2`) — a green local gate is not evidence about CI, the same class as "loopback is not the user's address" (2.366.1); ③ when the only signal for a failure goes to a human's inbox, it will be ignored for weeks — route it back to the agent that can act on it.
- CONFIG-WHITELIST STRIKE FOUR (2.368.1, owner-caught within hours of 2.368.0: picked an output style, resumed, chip still "默认"): `sidebar-state.setSessionConfig` whitelists persistable keys, and BOTH 2.368.0 writers (`outputStyle`, `autoResume`) were missing from it — the exact failure its own NOTE documents for `account` (2.43.0) and `groupManager` (2.132.0). The pick was accepted by the UI, silently dropped at persist, and the only symptom was an inert chip. Worse, the truthy filter (`if (config?.[k])`) would have erased an explicit `autoResume:false` even if whitelisted — the tri-state's per-session OFF (its entire reason to exist) cannot ride a truthy check. FIX: whitelist + an explicit `=== true || === false` branch for autoResume; the session records the EFFECTIVE spawn style (default-sourced included); a pick renders as PENDING on the chip (`Concise ⏳`) at pick time and at attach. INVARIANTS: ① adding a per-session config key = same commit touches the whitelist, and test-auto-resume pins it (a comment asking future authors to remember has now failed four times — only a test stops a fifth); ② booleans that mean "explicit OFF" never pass through truthy filters; ③ a saved-but-not-yet-effective choice must be VISIBLE — the silent drop was only diagnosable because the owner noticed nothing changed.
- HOSTED PAGE NEEDED A SECURE CONTEXT (2.366.1, owner: "打开后无法加载" then "你用的是你发给我的链接访问的吗？"): a published design canvas rendered its chrome and then hung EVERY artboard on "Loading artboard…" forever, for the owner and for anyone not on loopback. Root cause: **`crypto.randomUUID` is gated on a SECURE CONTEXT**; VibeSpace is reached over plain http on a hostname / LAN IP / Tailscale address, none of which are potentially-trustworthy, so `randomUUID` is `undefined` and the canvas editor (which mints ids with it) never finishes mounting an artboard. It worked over `127.0.0.1` because Chrome treats loopback as trustworthy — so every test I ran on loopback was meaningless, and I asserted "renders perfectly" three times before the owner asked whether I had used the link I gave them (I had not). Two decoys cost most of the diagnosis: the owner's console was full of extension scripts dying on `Invalid target origin 'null'` and of `localStorage` SecurityErrors — BOTH real consequences of the sandbox CSP's opaque origin, NEITHER the thing that hung the page. FIX: a serve-time COMPAT PRELUDE on `/p/` content (polyfill `randomUUID` from `getRandomValues` — a real v4, never Math.random; in-memory `localStorage`/`sessionStorage` when the opaque origin makes the real ones throw; each installed ONLY when genuinely missing, so https/loopback keep the browser's own) + a SHELL/RAW split: `/p/<id>` is our own trivial HTML on the real origin framing `/p/<id>/raw`, where the content still runs under the same sandbox CSP (never `allow-same-origin`) — isolation identical, top-level origin real, extensions no longer crash. INVARIANTS: ① **verify a hosted artifact at the address the USER will use** — loopback is a secure context and a LAN hostname is not, so `crypto.*`, storage and every secure-context-gated API behave differently there; a loopback pass proves nothing; ② content we host but did not write may depend on capabilities the deployment lacks — polyfill at the hosting layer, never edit the artifact; ③ when two error storms appear, check whether either can actually STOP the page before adopting one as the cause (both of these were noise); ④ every browser measurement must carry a NONCE (a query string echoed back from `location.search`) — three of my measurements were stale-tab artifacts that "confirmed" two different wrong root causes; ⑤ the fix must be proven by an A/B on ONE origin with the capability toggled (without polyfill 5/5 artboards stuck, with it 0/5), not by "it looks fine now".
- SHARE URL GUESSED FROM SOMEONE ELSE'S BROWSER (2.366.1, same session; owner: "你怎么知道我用啥地址能访问你？你怎么知道是不是存在反代？"): 2.366.0 remembered "the origin a browser last used" and returned it as the share URL — including to an AGENT, which then wrote `http://<this box's hostname>:3456/p/…` into a chat reply. A stored origin is a guess about a device that is not asking; behind a reverse proxy or a tunnel it is wrong by construction. FIX: an absolute URL comes only from `agentd.publicUrl` or from the Host of the request that is ASKING (browser routes only, because that browser will open it); agents get the RELATIVE path, the CLI prints it with an explicit "do not prefix a hostname", and the chat renderer linkifies `/p/<id>` against `location.origin`. INVARIANT: the server does not know how it is reached — only the asking browser does; never persist an origin to hand to a third party.
- GUARD ON THE PATH THAT MUST RUN (2.366.1, owner: chip said "1" but the popover only offered to create a new design): `_refillDesignList()` returns early on a detached node — correct for broadcasts arriving with no popover open — but the popover's INITIAL fill called it before appending the list, so it never populated. INVARIANT: a staleness guard belongs on the async/late path; the constructing path must run after the node is connected (the 2.306.0 "guard the live path" class again).
- CAPABILITY GATE READ THE WRONG FILE (2.364.1, owner: "一直在提示我wrapper太老让我terminate并resume，三次，我都照做了，中间还更新了一次vibespace"): the 2.361.1 frame-file capability gate decided whether a chat-wrapper understands `_frame_file` pointer lines by reading `caps.frameFile` from `readSessionMeta(sockName)` = `data/session-meta/<sock>.json` — the SERVER's session record (webuiSessionId/claudeSessionId/account…), which never carries caps. The wrapper writes its caps into its SIDECAR `data/session-buffers/<id>.json` (what boot-restore, the stuck-thinking heal and wrapper-files.resolveWrapperFiles all read). Consequences: every wrapper tested "old" regardless of age → the bypass was DEAD for two releases (data/chat-frames has zero files after the 2.361.1 commit time) → every >1MB paste was refused → the refusal text ("long-running wrapper started before the update — Terminate + Resume") sent the owner through three restarts and one update that could not help, then into terminal mode. It was invisible to diagnosis because (a) the refusal left NO server-side log line (the journal for the whole window is empty) and (b) the guard suite pinned the gate by TEXT (`/caps\?\.frameFile/` regex on ws-handler) — a pin that matched the wrong-file read perfectly. The first diagnosis in this very incident repeated the mistake: it read the refusal's own claim ("wrapper predates the update") as the explanation, found an old wrapper that fit, and stopped — the owner's "I resumed three times" was the falsifier. FIX: `wrapperCaps(BUFFERS_DIR, id, sockPath)` in src/server/wrapper-files.js is the ONE capability reader (sidecar via the collision-aware resolver, stateless, returns {frameFile, reason ok|no-caps|no-sidecar, startedAt, pid}); the gate caches only a POSITIVE verdict (`!== true` ⇒ re-read; a wrapper still booting a 789MB resume has no sidecar yet and must not be locked out by its first read); the refusal carries EVIDENCE (size, the wrapper's start time, or "still starting — wait a moment") and logs `chat-input REFUSED` to the journal. INVARIANTS: ① a capability a process advertises is read from the file THAT process writes, through its canonical resolver — never from a neighbouring record sharing the id (session-meta ≠ sidecar; two files, two writers); ② negative capability verdicts are never cached (absence is temporal, presence is permanent for a process lifetime); ③ a refusal names its evidence (which wrapper, started when, why) — a refusal that only restates its own hypothesis misdirects the user into "fixing" the wrong thing; ④ gate tests run the reader against the file the REAL writer produced (test-chat-frame-guard §2 spawns data/bin/chat-wrapper.js, then calls wrapperCaps on its output) plus a negative control with the wrong-file shape present — text pins on the reader prove nothing about which file it reads; ⑤ when an incident report contradicts the explanation ("did that three times, still happens"), the explanation is wrong — re-derive from the code path, not from the error text.
- PROXIED POST EATEN BY THE BODY PARSER (2.363.2, inc-mt2bpw2f, userW: "用Proxy打开这个网页，里面的交互按钮点不动"): app.use(express.json) sat ABOVE app.use(unblocker), so a proxied JSON POST's body stream was consumed by the parser before unblocker forwarded it — the target received headers with a Content-Length and waited for a body that never came, until the page's fetch aborted. GETs bypass body parsing entirely, so proxied pages RENDERED perfectly while every interactive action hung — a half-working page that reads as "the app is broken", not "the transport is broken". Repro'd deterministically (isolated express+unblocker harness + headless chrome clicking a POST button: AbortError with the parser first, post-ok without). FIX: the json parser skips /proxy/ paths (`req.path.startsWith('/proxy/') ? next() : jsonBody(...)`); unblocker deliberately stays mounted AFTER auth.middleware — moving it up would have been the obvious fix and an open-proxy/SSRF hole. INVARIANTS: streaming middleware (proxies, tunnels, upload relays) must see the RAW request — global body parsers and any pass-through route are mutually exclusive, and a new global parser must audit every streaming consumer below it; "renders but won't interact" through any relay ⇒ suspect the method split (GET path vs body-carrying path) before suspecting the page. Gate: scripts/test-proxy-post.mjs (6, real unblocker module).
- SEND REFUSAL READ AS ATTACH FAILURE (2.363.1, inc-mt2arppw, userW: "发消息就会直接中断，然后就得resume"): chat-view's per-session error handler treated EVERY {type:'error', sessionId} as an attach failure → view-only rescue → the LIVE window flipped into the Resume bar; AND the server's chat-input refusals (poison guard / too-large-for-old-wrapper) put their actionable text in `error:` while the handler read `msg.message` — so a CORRECT refusal ("…NOT sent. Terminate + Resume…") displayed as a dead-looking window with no reason, and the incident ring recorded msg:"" (it too read only .message), hiding the story from forensics. The session was never broken — attach always succeeded, the loop was user-visible-only. FIX: refusal sites send code:'input-rejected' + text in BOTH fields; ChatView renders coded refusals in-chat (✗ card) BEFORE the rescue branch; ring captures .error too. INVARIANTS: one ws error TYPE serving multiple semantics needs a CODE the client branches on — "error while attached" ≠ "error attaching", and conflating them turns every refusal into a fake crash; error payloads use ONE field name end-to-end (message), or every reader carries the belt; a diagnostic ring that drops the payload field is a forensic hole (this hunt cost an hour to a msg:"" entry). Gate: test-chat-frame-guard §5 pins (24). **ROUND 2 (2026-09-06, caught in review before shipping): the fix's own generalization was a REGRESSION.** Adding the queue-op refusal, the rule was widened to "any per-session `error` carrying a `code` is a scoped refusal" — which silently swallowed `ended-during-attach` (ws-handler raises it when the CLI dies WHILE its history rebuilds): a coded error whose session really is gone, which must keep taking the view-only rescue + Resume bar, or a dead session renders as a live-looking empty window. The rule is now an EXPLICIT allow-list (`SCOPED_REFUSAL_CODES` = input-rejected / not-codex-chat / queue-op-unsupported) plus an opt-in marker the SERVER can set (`scope:'action'`), and `_onSessionError` is a prototype method so both meanings are driven functionally (test-queue-steer ⑦). INVARIANT: "carries a code" is a fact about the WIRE, not about severity — a discriminator you generalize must be one whose values you enumerate; when the safe default is the destructive branch, opt IN to the gentle one.
- PEER MESSAGE INVISIBLE ON THE LIVE STREAM (2.362.2, inc-mt27t0bg, userW: 收端会话"没有一个 highlight 展示…来自谁谁谁的消息和消息本身的内容没有显示"): when the CLI drains a cross-session inbox delivery (harness SendMessage / vibespace-msg / job notify), the stream-json STDOUT stream NEVER carries the user record with the sender's words — verified against real 2.1.233 AND 2.1.235 buffers: stdout gets command_lifecycle started/completed + an init re-emit + the turn's assistant/tool records + a terminal `result` whose `origin` field carries the full envelope {kind:'peer', msg_id, name, fromMode, body}; the user record itself is JSONL-only (written ~13ms after enqueue). So the 2.349.0/2.361.6 peer card only ever rendered on JSONL rebuilds (restart/resume) — a LIVE-attached window showed the agent replying to nothing, and frequent dev restarts on the owner instance masked the gap for weeks. FIX: `_processResult` mines `result.origin.kind==='peer'` (body non-empty — real body-less origins exist: {kind:'peer',from:'unknown'}) and synthesizes the peer card at turn end (late but visible live; true order on any rebuild); dedup = `_peerMsgIds` Set noted at ALL THREE peer sites (user record, queued_command attachment, result rung); msg_id is AUTHORITATIVE when present — the last-12-messages text-containment fallback applies ONLY to msg_id-less legacy records (review-caught pre-ship: AND-ing it unconditionally suppressed every repeat fire of a same-body recurring notify and any short body ⊂ recent typed text — the very symptom re-introduced); mining precedes the is_error branch so interrupted turns still surface the message. INVARIANTS: the live stdout stream and the JSONL are DIFFERENT record sets — "renders after refresh/restart" vs "renders live" must both be tested for any record class (this is the 2.88.0 queued_command lesson in the opposite direction); when a stream omits a record, its terminal `result.origin` may be the only carrier — mine it, dedup'd by msg_id, never by assuming the primary record arrived. Gate: scripts/test-peer-msg-card.mjs (19, incl. same-body/distinct-msg_id and short-body⊂typed negative controls — a dedup test whose cases vary EVERY key at once proves nothing). SECOND LAYER (2.363.0, owner report the day after): SERVER-posted deliveries (Background Work notify / vibespace-msg — peer-messaging.js posting as an UNREGISTERED peer) get a CLI origin of {kind:'peer',from:'unknown'} with NO name/msg_id/body — the result.origin is body-less, so mining has nothing and even 2.362.2 stayed blank for this class. The server IS the poster ⇒ the delivery ladder renders the card itself on every ok lane (conversation-deliver emitPeerCard → normalizer injectPeerCard, deliberately containment-free), stash drains emit the same card at injection (4 sites), and peerDisplayName parses the server frames for rebuild parity. INVARIANT: a message channel's VISIBILITY must be owned by the party that HAS the information — when the transport strips attribution (unregistered poster), the poster renders, never hope the receiver can. THIRD LAYER (codex, design-harness-plugins §1 P1): CodexMessageManager had no injectPeerCard at all, so `feedPeerCard` returned false for EVERY codex session — auto-resume notices, Background Work drains and vibespace-msg cards were dropped without a trace, and the rpc-queue lane's wrapper-recorded user message rendered as an anonymous "You" bubble. Fix: codex `injectPeerCard` (same card shape as claude's); the rpc frame carries fromName+cardText and the wrapper writes them as a `webui_peer:{name,body}` marker into ITS OWN buffer record (the party holding the information) — `recordKey` + `mergeCodexRecords` strip the marker so the buffer copy keeps colliding with codex's rollout copy of the same user message (pinned in BOTH timestamp orders: the idle path writes the rollout copy first, the queued path the wrapper copy first), and a rollout-only rebuild recognises the ANCHORED server frame through the shared `peerDisplayName`. Rejected alternative: "the deliver lane calls feedPeerCard for codex too" — the wrapper record + an in-memory card double-render live, and the card would fire before the wrapper confirms delivery (ok:false re-stashes and re-renders at drain ⇒ a phantom card). INVARIANTS: a marker one transport adds to its copy of a twin record must be stripped by EVERY fingerprint that dedups the twins, and the test must vary which twin survives; a mid-turn peer record must never finalize the active turn's streams, and a peer card is never a turn boundary; typed records (webui_msg_id) are never peer cards whatever their text looks like. Gate: test-peer-msg-card (52) + test-peer-delivery (21).
- NOTIFY-CRON NEVER WOKE ITS OWNER AGENT (2.361.5, the 设备运维大师 relay's final layer): the notify ACTION called only d.notifyUser (user inbox/toast) + a passive group event — _notifyOwner/_deliverTo were never invoked for action-type notify, so an agent's self-scheduled reminder (the dated-obligation pattern the bookkeeping rules themselves prescribe) reached the human's inbox and the group's passive context line but NEVER the owning conversation. Diagnosed from the delivery topology, not the knobs (urgency=low / notify=inherit were innocent — the lane simply wasn't wired). FIX: notify fires ride the standard owner-delivery stack (notifyEffective toggles → 30s rate floor → live peer message → stash) in addition to the user lane; plus a per-job `notifyLog` ring (every ATTEMPT: lane/ok/to/reason, cap 12, panel-rendered as "Delivery log") so delivery is OBSERVABLE — the debugging here consisted entirely of reconstructing invisible outcomes, which the journal now makes a 5-second read. INVARIANTS: every notification-shaped feature must state its DELIVERY TOPOLOGY explicitly (who gets woken, who gets a passive line, who gets an inbox item) — "notify" without a recipient model breeds phantom-channel hunts; delivery attempts leave a journal (telemetry having it ≠ the owner can see it — the no-silent-failures law applied to the notification system itself).
- RUN+ECHO REMINDER = SILENT NO-OP + MISLEADING CREATE RESPONSE (2.361.4, the other half of 79928a2b's reminder hunt, owner-directed): scheduling a bare echo IS the natural agent instinct for a reminder, but the quiet-success law (notifyOk=false) made that shape a permanent no-op — worse, the creation response's "auto-notify: ON — messaged on completion/failure" reads as per-fire notification (it means terminal/park/ask events). FIX: scheduled pure echo/printf (regex-conservative: no pipes/chains/substitution) defaults notifyOk=true with an explanatory line; every scheduled task creation now states quiet-success semantics explicitly. INVARIANT (pairs with the resolved-time echo of 2.361.3): a creation response must state the semantics the CREATOR most plausibly assumed — "what will I be told, and when" for anything scheduled; when a common instinct-shape maps to a silently-useless configuration, either make the instinct work or refuse loudly, never accept-and-ignore.
- AGENT-TIME IS UTC, BARE DATETIMES ARE SERVER-LOCAL (2.361.3, 79928a2b's cron-notification hunt): agents' entire visible world is UTC (transcript timestamps, task logs), so when one computes "now + 2 minutes" it writes a UTC wall time — and `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM")` parses bare strings in the server's LOCAL timezone, scheduling the job hours out. The agent then observes "notification never arrived" and diagnoses a broken delivery channel (its actual earlier misconfig — run+echo with notifyOk=false success-silence — it had already found itself; the cron ENGINE was firing on time throughout, runs accumulate in the reused "<name> run" child job). FIX: --at accepts relative "+2m"/"+1h30m" (the form agents should always prefer), creation echoes `next fire: <ISO> (in Xm)` so a tz mis-parse is caught at create time, help/manual teach the trap. INVARIANT: any agent-facing time input must either be relative, carry an explicit zone, or ECHO its resolved absolute time back — silent local-tz interpretation of an agent-supplied wall time is a footgun by construction.
- MONTHLY-SPEND-CAP REJECT UNMARKED (2.361.2, live same-night): once an org has extra usage, the CLI enforces the weekly lane on OVERAGE-INCLUDED accounting — rate_limit_event carries `seven_day_overage_included`, which rate-limit-capture mapped to 'other' (surfaced, never marked). A member with weekly spent + org monthly spend cap exhausted (`overageDisabledReason: org_level_disabled_until`, banner "You've hit your monthly spend limit") stayed at 7d=0.53 in cache and the pool re-picked it after the banner's 5h mark rolled. FIX: map the type to sevenDay (rejected → dead until the EVENT resetsAt; warnings → enforced-lane utilization reading; overage fields land in cache.overage). INVARIANTS: a bucket-type allowlist in a capture path is a silent-drop trap — every newly observed rateLimitType must either map or FAIL LOUDLY in a gate test (the api_retry lesson, third strike); "monthly limit" is NOT a new quota bucket — it is the extra-usage monthly credit budget (org spend cap), an ACCOUNT-level dead-until state; the pool's liveness model still lacks the spend-cap dimension as a first-class signal (parked: consume cache.overage.disabledReason as account-dead-until in verdicts).
- WRAPPER VERSION SKEW ATE IMAGE PASTES (2.361.1, c1206711, same-day catch; **NOTE: the gate's reader pointed at the wrong file until 2.364.1 — see CAPABILITY GATE READ THE WRONG FILE above**): 2.360.0's frame-file bypass assumed every session's chat-wrapper understands `_frame_file` pointer lines — but wrappers are LONG-LIVED processes (dtach survives updates), so every session spawned before the update forwarded the pointer VERBATIM to claude, which silently dropped the unknown type: the user's image+text message vanished from the UI on refresh (never reached the transcript — zero queue-operation records) while the full content sat orphaned in data/chat-frames/. FIX: wrapper advertises `caps: { frameFile: true }` in its boot meta; ws-handler consults it (session._wrapperFrameFile, schema-registered) and only capability-advertising wrappers get pointer lines; capability-less wrappers keep raw stdin ≤1MB (the historically-safe single-screenshot range) and a VISIBLE refusal error above it; orphans swept at 48h. Note the graduation window: sessions spawned by 2.360.0/2.361.0 servers run frame-file-capable wrappers WITHOUT the marker — they are treated conservatively (raw/refuse) until recreated, which is correct-direction degradation. INVARIANT (capability-gate law extended to wrappers): any NEW stdin protocol the server speaks to a session must be gated on a capability the session's RUNNING wrapper advertised — mirror of the device-op rule "old daemons are never asked — unknown ops HANG", and the second member of the "long-lived process keeps its loaded code" class alongside the CLI stale-token/stale-binary essays. Recovery for a hit session needs no server update: Terminate → Resume respawns the wrapper from current data/bin code.
- HOT-SWITCH STALE-TOKEN MISATTRIBUTION → OTel TRUTH CHANNEL (2.361.0, B-345b 终案): the dead-reckoning ≥10pt burst layer under-estimated -30~-49% and would NOT converge after 2.340.0. Mechanisms tested and REFUTED before the real one (archive — do not re-investigate): training censoring (73 big pairs WERE in the learning set, 42% of learned Δu), output-token share (3-4% both strata), model-family mix (fable ~90% both), cw5/cw1 TTL mix (30/70 vs 20/80), per-request overhead ($/req equal), unresolved-host exclusion ($0), walker gaps (raw-transcript recount diff = $0 — the ledger fully covers local spend), reading lag (storm-envelope residual doesn't cancel), cross-instance spend (owner ground truth: none exists). ROOT CAUSE: pool hot-switch does not reach a RUNNING CLI — repointPoolSymlink's utimes/mtime trick only affects new processes or token refresh; forensic case 79928a2b was auto-switched across 4 orgs in 35min while its process kept billing the ORIGINAL org to 95% and rate-limited from it (the rate_limit_event came from that session). 558 mid-session switches / $7.9k of ≤30min-post-switch spend in 12 days meant link-intent attribution scrambled storm-time cost in both directions → per-org odometers wrong AND learned rates attenuated. FIX = consume the only channel that NAMES the billing org per request: the CLI's OTel api_request export → loopback receiver → rid-exact bake override + corrective attribution + stash (kb-file-structure: otel-truth.js / server/otel-ingest.js). INVARIANTS: link-intent attribution is NEVER billing truth while hot-switching exists — per-request identity comes from the truth channel; transcripts/statusline/rate_limit_event are confirmed identity-free (like /status, the 2026-08-05 essay); calibration audits must stratify by movement size AND reconcile at storm level (slice-level residuals hide inside storms).
- SCHEMA-TWIN FALSY-0 REVERT (2.360.1, inc-mt0mozsp): the owner set both stop-nudge numbers to 0 ("every stop" mode, a first-class semantic since 2.210.0 — schema min: 0, server clamp0 passes explicit 0) and watched them jump back to 10/30 on Save. Root cause was TWO stacked defects in the Manage Agents → Agent instructions tab (manage-agents.js), which is a SECOND editing surface for the same settings the Settings window handles correctly: ① a hardcoded pre-2.210.0 bounds/default map `{staleMinutes:[1,240,10], cooldownMinutes:[2,720,30]}` — a schema TWIN that never learned min moved to 0, so even a surviving 0 would clamp up to 1/2; ② `Number(inp.value) || dflt` — the falsy-0 coalescing erased the 0 before the clamp even ran. Forensics: the incident bundle's action ring showed the user in `agents-cond-num` inputs + the agents-adv Save button (NOT the Settings window), three settings-updated echoes, and data/settings.json missing both overrides afterward. FIX: the dialog reads `SETTINGS_SCHEMA[key].min/max/default` (twin deleted), and Save distinguishes "empty/non-numeric → default" from "explicit 0 → 0". INVARIANTS (arch asserts #41-42): UI code never re-declares a schema row's numbers inline — bounds/defaults have ONE home (settings-schema.js); number-input handling never uses `|| default` (explicit 0 is a value — the same trap settings-ui.js already avoided with Number.isFinite). The class to grep for when adding any second editing surface: every constant it embeds is a twin that will drift.
- 38MB TRANSCRIPT POISONING VIA PTY-SHREDDED IMAGE PASTE (2.360.0, owner's 设备运维大师 79928a2b): a 5-image chat paste builds ONE multi-MB stream-json frame; that single line does NOT survive the node-pty→dtach→wrapper stdin channel — mid-line bytes AND the newline get dropped, the shredded frame glues onto the next send, JSON.parse fails in formatChatInput, and the else branch WRAPS THE SHREDS AS TEXT into the conversation → a 38MB user record = every later API call rejected (session reads as dead) + the huge-session 32MB tail window lands MID-LINE (history renders blank; the user thinks it's lost — it never was). THREE-LAYER FIX, all pinned in scripts/test-chat-frame-guard.mjs (ci gate): ① frame-file bypass — ws-handler writes frames >64KB to data/chat-frames/ and stdin carries only a `_frame_file` pointer line; the wrapper reads, validates it as ONE frame (a shredded file is DROPPED, never wrapped), forwards, unlinks; local claude chat only (remote wrappers can't see this fs — their large pastes still rely on ②); ② poison guard — formatChatInput's wrap-as-text branch THROWS on >512KB blobs that look like frames; ws-handler surfaces it as a per-session `{type:'error'}` toast; ③ RESCUE (productized surgery, owner request): transcripts.rescue / POST /api/session-rescue / session-card "Rescue transcript…" (stopped local sessions) — streams the file (never blocks the loop), stubs every >1MB record's message content IN PLACE (same uuid/parent = chain+order+count untouched), byte-preserved .pre-rescue backup, only rewritten lines re-validated, atomic swap; refuses while any writer holds the file (fuser). Invariant: the wrap-as-text fallback is a POISON PATH for anything huge — any new stdin channel work must keep the guard in front of it.
- VERBATIM-COPY CASE TYPO IN A GENERATED SCRIPT (2.359.1, owner's Ctrl+G outage — broken 35 releases since the 2.325.0 拆分): the decomp commit 9e42417 moved the editor-helper template "verbatim" but turned bash `\${PORT}` into `\${port}` — inside a JS template literal where escaped `\${…}` is bash and unescaped `${…}` is JS, the case slip is invisible to every static gate (acorn no-undef sees no JS identifier; bash -n sees a legal script). The generated `code` helper curl'd an empty port and waited on its signal file forever. Invariants: ① generated scripts need CONTENT-level gates — restore-smoke now checks the worktree-boot-generated files: every `${lowercase}` bash ref must have an in-script definition + the editor URL must ride `${PORT}`; ② templates that mix JS interpolation with escaped shell vars are a "verbatim copy" trap — when extracting one, diff the GENERATED OUTPUT before/after, not the source text; ③ a helper that waits on a signal file must have a timeout-with-message (still open — parked).
- UNSTAGED WIRING — A GREEN UNIT TEST OVER A DEAD FIX (2.355.0, userW's inc-msy27q2e repeating his own 2.331.0 report): commit 52eb46c shipped `scanStoppedInDesktopStates` (the resume-all-covers-every-desktop fix) + its unit suite, but the `loadAutoSave` CALL SITE was never staged — the pure function sat exported-but-uncalled for 24 releases while `test-resume-all-desktops` stayed green, because a pure function's test proves the LOGIC, not the WIRING. Invariants: ① every "pure fn + test" fix needs a WIRING PIN in the same suite (grep: the defining module must CALL the function ≥1 time beyond its definition — now in test-resume-all-desktops, which also joined the ci gate; it was manual-only); ② the 验证过的build≠提交的内容 rule extends to NEW code, not just edits — after committing a fix, grep the COMMIT DIFF for both the definition AND a call site (`git show --stat` touching only the defining file + tests = red flag).
- MANUAL POOL HOT-SWITCH DEMOTED BY PLAN C (2.355.0, userW's inc-msz495u6 "热切换死了"): per-session links (2.315.0) take precedence in poolCurrentFor, but setPoolTarget only moved the pool DEFAULT symlink — a manual target change silently became new-sessions-only (verified live on the reporting instance: default → new member, 16 live session links → old member; his pool auto:false hot:true, so nothing would ever move them). Fix: the USER route passes {sweepSessionLinks} (gated on pool.hot) and setPoolTarget repoints every live link + logs the count; the ENGINE never passes it (its per-session moves are the model-family projections). Invariant: when a NEW indirection layer (per-session links) takes precedence over an OLD control surface (the manual switch), every writer of the old surface must be re-audited as a consumer of the new one — the layer demoted the control silently and no test caught it because both tests lived on their own side of the layer.
- FREE-IDENTIFIER SWEEP ×8 + SERVER NO-UNDEF GATE (2.343.2, owner-requested "再检查下还有没有类似的"): a 3-agent audit + deterministic scans found EIGHT more latent breaks — classifyCliDeath/https/os/adapterRegistry/ClaudeCodeAdapter free identifiers, agentdDeps ghost binding ×2 (mounts-plugins-wiring AND server.js ssh→dial auto-graduation, which had silently never fired), persistenceRouter free in incident-wiring (bundles lost layouts), and the allow-exit hosts-updated broadcast deleted as collateral (stale/inverting toggle). Root pattern: the server had NO no-undef gate (the client has had one since 2.330.1). Invariants: ①scripts/test-server-globals.mjs (acorn scope analysis, all server-tier files, in npm run ci) makes the whole class build-impossible — it caught the 8th find on its own first run; ②degrade catches around these were mostly SILENT (bare catch{}) — the 2.284 verbatim-log rule remains the difference between weeks-latent and found-same-day.
- DEAD-CODE SWEEP DELETED A LIVE METHOD (2.343.1, publish layer 2): the dc37220 audit batch removed PluginManager._notify() as "dead" while 8 in-file call sites remained — plugin broadcasts + publish threw for weeks, unseen (no battery exercises plugin mutation paths). Invariants: ①deleting "dead" code requires grepping the SAME file for call sites, not just cross-file references; ②test-architecture #9 now statically requires every server-side `this._x()` call to have an in-file definition (src/lib mixins excluded — prototype methods live across files there).
- PROXY-SWALLOWED ASSIGNMENT (2.343.0, 7th of the decomposition class: owner's publish report): lazy.js mk() Proxy had get-only traps — `portForwards.plugins = plugins` in mounts-plugins-wiring wrote to the dummy target, the real singleton never got the handle, publish dead fleet-wide for 17 releases with a misleading "not available on this instance" error. No throw anywhere = invisible to every battery. Invariants: ①mk() carries a forwarding set trap (scripts/test-lazy.mjs pins reads/binds/ASSIGNMENTS/re-created-singleton writes); ②extraction checklist: any `x.prop = value` where x crosses a module seam must verify x is the real instance, not a bridge; ③a capability-missing error message must distinguish "handle never wired" from "feature unconfigured" — the misleading error cost the diagnosis.
- RELATIVE-PATH LOST BINDING (2.341.1, 6th of the class, first RELATIVE-PATH: userW's dial-mount outage): extraction #13 (拆分) carried `require('./package.json')` from server.js into src/server/dial-pairing.js verbatim — the spec is syntactically fine, resolves against the NEW directory, and throws "Cannot find module" only when the dial path RUNS (`deviceForDial` = every fresh-DeviceManager dial op incl. machine mounts; `ensureAgentdOnHost` = ssh agentd install). No free identifier, so the static free-variable check was blind; call-time, so boot smokes and the route battery were blind; the error DID surface to the user (no-silent-failures held). Invariants: ①when moving code between directories, every relative require/import moves its ANCHOR — re-resolve them as part of the extraction checklist; ②test-architecture #8 (permanent, in `npm run build`) statically resolves every relative require/import under server.js+src/ (.js/.json/.mjs/index.js candidates) and fails on a dangling one — negative-controlled against this exact file; the class is now build-impossible.
- LATENT LOST BINDING (2.340.2, 5th of the class, first LATENT): parseRateLimitEvent/captureRateLimitEvent were free identifiers in usage-pool-engine.js since extraction #5 — recordRateLimitEvent's try/catch swallowed the ReferenceError into `[usage] rate_limit_event capture failed: …` (48 log lines over 3 days, found only while diagnosing an unrelated stuck session). Passive rate_limit capture (limit→pool reflex + rate_limit anchors) was dead the whole time and every gate stayed green — the boot/route batteries can't see a runtime throw that a catch eats. Invariants: ①a degrade-path catch must log the message VERBATIM (2.284 rule — sole reason this was found) AND such logs must be GREPPED after every decomposition (`journalctl | grep -iE 'failed:.*is not defined'`); ②the restore-smoke could assert zero '*is not defined' lines in its boot output — worth adding when touching extraction seams.
- STUCK-THINKING AFTER RESTART / COUNTER-COLLISION FILE MISMATCH (2.339.2, 设备运维大师 real report): boot-restore derived the wrapper sidecar/buffer paths from the SOCKET name, but 2.302.0-era counter-collision sessions carry a different id in the wrapper argv — every restart read nonexistent files into catch{} and silently lost streaming/goal/todos/agent-watchers/buffer for those sessions. Invariants: ①any read of `<id>.{buf,json}` for a LIVE session must go through src/server/wrapper-files.js resolveWrapperFiles (socket-process argv is the authority when the expected pair is missing); ②the wrapper sidecar's `streaming` field is the turn-state authority across restarts — the ws attach reconciles a server-believed mid-turn against a settled (>3s) sidecar saying false and heals (missed-result class can never show thinking forever); ③counter-collision residue is PERMANENT in long-lived fleets — never assume socket name == wrapper id.
- TYPING-DRIVEN PAGING SELF-FEED (2.338.0, Windows freeze audit; the round-5 disease through the uncovered door): chat-input autosize per-keystroke write→read→write + real wraps shrinking the scroller → content-visibility re-resolution + scroll anchoring drifted scrollTop with ZERO user input → `_extendTop` (which unlike extendBottom had NO positive-evidence gate) paged 50 messages per bounce until history exhausted — a finite seconds-to-30s freeze that self-recovers, NOT a loop. Fixes carry invariants: ①every extend branch (top AND bottom) requires recent real user input — displacement is never intent, and new entry points (input resize, cv resolution, anchoring) must be assumed until proven absent; ②chat-input autosize stays rAF-coalesced with a no-op skip and stamps `__vsInputResizeAt` (250ms displacement guard both extends read); ③`_forceScrollToBottom` is a SINGLE refreshed-countdown chain — never one chain per op (overlapping chains = N forced layouts/frame); ④the tail-append runs pass is rAF-coalesced (rAF runs pre-paint so the 2.227.9 no-flash guarantee holds); ⑤streaming full-text re-parse throttled ≥150ms; ⑥code-block DOM capped 3000 lines + plain output skips the span-carry walk; ⑦reconnect epoch resets and catch-ups stagger 0-500ms per window; ⑧boot restore runs under wm._restoring (one bookkeeping pass at end); ⑨WebGL contexts capped at 12 (Windows/ANGLE context-burst = GPU-process stall = whole-page freeze); ⑩the 45s wake detector votes suspend-vs-block by SUM of longtasks (freeze shape is many medium tasks).
- **Three-tier closure marathon (2.297.0–2.300.0, owner directive "一次性完全做完"; invariants, do not regress)**: ① **scripts/test-vendor-whitelist.mjs is the structural §ban-safety law** — exactly two files may construct vendor requests (pinned counts + gate markers), the device protocol's vendor surface is EXACTLY the quota-refresh op (humanGated hard-refusal + throttle stamped ONLY before a real vendor call (2.271.0 rule) + read-only peek), shipped usage tools import no network primitives; a new vendor call anywhere fails the suite until deliberately allowlisted WITH its gates. ② **Offline-bias defenses**: usageHistory.sourceWatermarks() (per-machine newest-event ts, free on the shard walk) + hosts.linkState() (never probes) → darkSources()/darkTaintedAccounts() (server.js, 30s memo) → decidePoolSwitch `pessimism` docks a tainted account's remaining on BOTH sides (current trips exhaustion earlier AND a tainted candidate fails the settle bar); anchors record `dark:[hosts]` and extractPairs VOIDS pairs touching a dark interval (Δu real, cost missing ⇒ falsely hot rate). ③ **costBetweenMulti counts RESOLVED remote events** (only atype:'host' stays out) and **host-login remote sessions skip the noteLive ring** — the pre-2.297.0 pair made remote spend rise-then-un-count in the estimate. ④ **place-secret is THE secret channel on the device link** (0600 AT OPEN, atomic, $HOME-confined — the fsWrite-then-chmod pair left a world-readable window); old daemons keep the legacy pair as fallback. ⑤ **quota-refresh executes ON the login-holding machine** (usage-routes host branches prefer the op; server never sees the token; foreign-IP signal gone). ⑥ **sealed-orders**: the daemon executes a LOCAL pool fallback switch ONLY under the double condition (limit banner in live pipe stdout AND authedServers===0); banner parser = the bundled adapter's parseLimitBanner, re-point = account-material — ONE implementation each; executions ride the NEXT pool-orders REPLY (pushing at hello RACES the client's control-routing installation — observed live); server re-pushes orders at boot + every eval, hash-throttled. ⑦ **bufferOwner explicit in every session meta** ('server' today; attach routes by it when R6 session.open lands). ⑧ Session-brain steps 2–4 + R5's local-discovery leg are the SOAK-GATED remainder per the design's own sequencing rule — recorded in docs/design-three-tier.md §session-brain status, not silent debt. ⑨ Push-stream client additions need THREE touches or they silently no-op: the op in the id-keyed routing set, the unsolicited-push branch BEFORE prevControl, and the reconnect re-arm (the usage-events routing line was lost to an aborted batch edit and cost two debug cycles — the daemon-side failure LOG, added per the 2.284.2 no-silent-catch lesson, is what located it).
- **PAGING INTENT GATE (2.307.0, inc-msorcsrl — the mechanism two earlier fixes missed)**: after an upward page, content-visibility resolves the fresh batch and NATIVE SCROLL ANCHORING raises scrollTop to keep the view stable (captured: 85 → 1466 in 26 ms — no input device can do that). The `near the end ⇒ _extendBottom` rule read that displacement as the user scrolling DOWN, trimmed the top and walked the window back to the live tail. **scrollTop change ≠ user intent** — content edits, native anchoring and our own anchor restores all move it. Boundary EXTENSIONS now require agreement with the user's real wheel direction (`_wheelDir`/`_wheelDirAt`, 1.2 s expiry so keyboard/programmatic scrolling still pages); content growth has no direction and can no longer masquerade as intent. Diagnostic rule this cost three captures to learn: when a guard is demonstrably FIRING and the symptom is unchanged, the ASSUMPTION is wrong — go back to the data for the unexplained number, do not harden the existing explanation.
- **PAGING BOUNCE / COLLAPSED GEOMETRY (2.301.0→2.306.0, inc-mso818ry + inc-msor3oax)**: while content-visibility leaves a freshly paged batch unresolved the list collapses to `scrollHeight === clientHeight`, making "at top" and "at bottom" the SAME position. 2.301.0 guarded the SCROLL handler — and the recurrence capture shows that guard FIRING while the bounce continued, because **a collapsed list emits no scroll events at all**: the WHEEL handler is the only live path there, and its bottom-edge branch was unguarded (one `deltaY>0` — the momentum tail of an UPWARD gesture — hit `_extendBottom` and yanked the window to the live tail, ~1/s). The bottom branch now requires a REAL scrollable range (>50px); the scroll guard is "scrollable range < one viewport AND within 1.5s of a structural mutation" (`_lastStructuralAt`, stamped at extendTop/extendBottom/trimTop/trimBottom) — a fixed 200px bar missed the field landings (782/923/997 against a 755px list) and simply widening it would make a genuinely short partial window skip decisions FOREVER. RULE: before guarding an event, verify that event still fires in the failure state, and cover every entry point that computes the same boundary (scroll/wheel/touch/observer). Test: scripts/test-paging-collapse-guard.mjs (26, field numbers).
- **SCOPED-BUCKET MERGE (2.305.0, inc-msof8i22 — the pool refused to leave an account whose Opus was spent)**: model-scoped weekly caps arrive EITHER as `model_scoped[]`/`limits[]` entries OR as named `seven_day_opus`-style fields, and REAL payloads carry BOTH with different members. Both parsers (`ClaudeCodeAdapter.parseGetUsageResponse` + usage-routes `_parseUsage`) treated the named fields as a fallback for an EMPTY array, so one Fable entry hid the Opus cap on every path — ten usage-cache files, zero Opus buckets, across ⟳/statusline/get_usage alike, and the pool's exhaustion test therefore never fired. They MERGE now (dedupe by model name, array entry wins). Regression must pin the BOTH-PRESENT shape; array-only and named-only both pass while the bug is live.
- **COUNTER CAPTURE (2.302.0, proven in fleet production data): a monotonic counter's value is CAPTURED ONCE and reused — any re-read after an `await` is a concurrency race.** ws-handler minted `id` with `++sessionCounterRef.value` but built `sockName` from a bare re-read ~100 lines and several awaits later (cwd preflight, resume host inference, findKeeperFor), so a burst of concurrent creates all took the LATEST value: 15 live sessions on a fleet instance, 7 with mismatched counters, four sharing `cw-36` **1 ms apart** — same-millisecond arrivals share a socket path AND a session-meta filename, so one session's metadata (name, claudeSessionId, account, taskId) silently overwrites another's. One `seq` now feeds id + sockName + default name (server.js adopt path too); scripts/test-session-id-race.mjs forbids bare re-reads structurally and reproduces the symptom as a negative control. Sibling fix: the client's `_migrateStateMap` silently DROPPED one of two values mapped onto the same session key (how a session ends up wearing another's custom name while the rightful owner loses its own) — now a `state-key-collision` breadcrumb + telemetry event.
- **B-b87b batch (2.267.0 — all 16 confirmed findings from the 31-agent global review; do not regress)**: ①dial-session-bridge pipe onExit = TRANSPORT death (a real child exit rides the `_remote_exit` sentinel) — it must tear the loopback down so the attach client respawns, never no-op (dial chat froze forever on any link drop). ②SettingsManager._save nulls `_saveTimer` in finally (guard covers the in-flight POST; a fired-timer id left armed made applyRemote/refetch skip every future remote settings change until reload). ③user-state pushes are DIFF-PATCHES against the last server-applied snapshot (`_serverStateJson`; new merge-only `PATCH /api/user-state`) — a stale tab can only send keys it changed; same-key races stay last-write-wins (documented residual). ④explicit account pick VETOES keeper/agentd/dial adoption (adopted children keep their original billing — the pick must respawn); adoption probes gate on `!data.accountId`. ⑤`resumeSpawn`/`sawFirstId` persist in session meta (implicit-fork adoption survives restarts). ⑥pooled account as DEFAULT on a remote create falls back to the host login; explicit pool pick on remote refuses (pools are local-only). ⑦remote spawns prepend `AMBIENT_OAT_UNSET` (all 5 command builders) — a host profile's CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN silently re-billed every remote session. ⑧ssh Terminate of agentd-ADOPTED sessions keys the state file by `session.keeperSid` (webui-id key = remote claude survived every terminate). ⑨pty-wrapper retry: `totalAttempts` monotonic cap + ladder reset only when the child LIVED ≥30s (ssh stderr is pty data — resetting on bytes made a dead host retry at 1s forever). ⑩chat-view _reattach: the sync handler stays armed for the WHOLE retry ladder (input re-enable is the only 30s concern); terminal ladder paths remove it. ⑪`parseArchiveListing` = the ONE listing parser (remote-fs exports, files.js imports) + RemoteFs.archiveExtractEntry/_streamCmdToFile/fetchToLocal (binary-safe both transports); ⑫/api/file/excel|csv|docx remote branches via `remoteToLocalTmp` (size+TTL-validated temp cache — the CSV pager re-reads ranges). ⑬dial ctx sync = `syncDialGroupCtx` (per-file newer-wins over the device link, CONTENT-HASHED both directions so fsWrite's mtime loss can't ping-pong; traversal guard on remote rel paths). ⑭client `_sessionFolderMatch` strips the display host label (board membership now agrees with server groupsForSession for remote sessions). ⑮fake `code` → data/bin/editor/ (see File Structure). ⑯billing-switcher rows render dead-reckoned estimates via estDisplayPair (dash-underlined) instead of stale raw readings.
- **Display cwd must never reach a spawn (2.225.2, userL h200 real incident)**: the sidebar merge composes remote webui sessions' cwd as the folder-grouping DISPLAY string `"<host name>: /path"` (a load-bearing grouping/archivedFolders KEY — do not change the composer); it leaked into persisted openSpecs and a resume `cd`'d into the literal label → claude ran from $HOME → "No conversation found" → 10min unresumable block. `stripCwdHostLabel` (utils.js — strips iff the remainder is an absolute/~ path) guards the client boundary (createSession/attachSession/viewSession) + a server-side twin at ws-handler create intake heals already-persisted specs and stale-bundle clients. Any NEW consumer that sends a merged-record cwd to the server must strip.
- **Restart-survival audit batch (2.218.0-2.219.0, user directive '整体review所有不抗重启处'; full 54-finding report: SharedContext/vibespace-restart-audit-20260721.json)**: ①resume host inference — a host-less claude resume with no local transcript but a unique data/remote-jsonl cache hit resumes ON that host;
